## New open findings — 2026-05-30 refresh (DOC-GAP-104..171)

Each finding renumbered sequentially in fragment order (B01 first). Full blocks preserved (category / severity / surfaced_by / evidence / proposed_doc_action / cross_references). Two cross-fragment duplicates were merged (B14-F1 → DOC-GAP-149; B13-F4 → DOC-GAP-140).

### DOC-GAP-104 (was B01-F1)
- category: drift (missing-caveat)
- severity: HIGH
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java auth handler:GithubUserHandler (`docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[2]` + `bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0]` + `security.known_security_gaps.[0]`)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/oauth2-oidc` 2026-05-29 status 200. The page was heavily rewritten and NOW documents three GitHub caveats previously absent (GHES-unsupported, admin-principals-bypasses-org-gate — both verbatim-present) — BUT on the username-rename risk it is explicitly NOT MENTIONED ("The documentation does not address the risk that GitHub username renames could orphan user-owner mappings when using `login` as the username attribute").
  - Code: `GithubUserHandler.java:36,56` — username is the GitHub `login` string (default `user-name-attribute: login`), NOT the stable numeric GitHub `id`. GitHub permits free login renames (old name held 90 days then released).
  - `AuthIdentityProviderImpl.java:29-30` + `ReactiveUserOwnerMappingRepositoryImpl.java:116-127` — the `(username, "github")` tuple is the compound key for `USER_OWNER_MAPPING`. A renamed user logs in as `(alice2, github)`; the prior `(alice, github)` mapping row is orphaned. No `id`-based fallback, no migration tool.
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "GitHub username renames break owner links" caveat to the GitHub section of `oauth2-oidc.md` (alongside the shipped GHES + admin-principals caveats): ODD identifies GitHub users by their mutable `login`; a rename creates a NEW identity and silently orphans the existing Owner association (My Objects goes empty, ownership stranded under the old name); no automatic re-link; plan a manual `USER_OWNER_MAPPING` migration around any GitHub login rename.
- cross_references: F-011 (compound-key-silent-in-docs general class; this is the GitHub-specific instance). Sibling cross-mode-bleed for LOGIN_FORM/LDAP is DOC-GAP-103 (distinct: provider=null bleed, not rename-orphan).

### DOC-GAP-105 (was B01-F2)
- category: drift (missing-caveat)
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java auth handler:GoogleUserHandler (`docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]` + `bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[3,4]`)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/oauth2-oidc` 2026-05-29 status 200. The page NOW documents the Google `admin-groups` silent-no-op, but on the `hd`-claim / personal-Gmail behaviour it is NOT MENTIONED ("only references `allowed-domain` property but does not explain the underlying `hd` (hosted-domain) claim mechanism or note that personal Gmail accounts cannot pass domain restrictions").
  - Code: `GoogleUserHandler.java:32,49-55` — `allowed-domain` is enforced by comparing the Google ID-token `hd` claim case-insensitively. The `hd` claim is emitted ONLY for Workspace accounts; personal `@gmail.com` accounts have no `hd`, so `equalsIgnoreCase(allowedDomain, null)` is false → login REJECTED by design.
  - `GoogleUserHandler.java:52-54` — surfaces to the user as `Domain null doesn't match with allowed domain <X>` (the `null` is interpolated for personal accounts).
- proposed_doc_action: Add to the Google section of `oauth2-oidc.md`: `allowed-domain` is enforced via the Google ID-token `hd` claim, emitted ONLY for Workspace accounts; personal `@gmail.com` accounts carry no `hd` and can NEVER sign in when `allowed-domain` is set (by design; failure reads `Domain null doesn't match with allowed domain ...`). Optionally cross-link a code follow-up to friendlier the null-domain error.
- cross_references: none (sibling Google admin-groups no-op already documented live; no prior DOC-GAP for the hd-claim gap).

### DOC-GAP-106 (was B02-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java auth logout:CognitoLogoutSuccessHandler (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[1] + bugs_limitations_corner_cases[2])
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/oauth2-oidc` 2026-05-29 status 200 (curl-confirmed). The Cognito section returned "NOT PRESENT" for any mention of AWS Cognito's "Allowed sign-out URLs" app-client allowlist requirement. The wave DID add a generic open-redirect caveat + a per-provider token-revocation matrix — but NOT the Cognito-allowlist operational consequence.
  - Code: `CognitoLogoutSuccessHandler.java:43` builds `logout_uri` from `UriUtils.getBaseUri(requestUri)` (inbound Host-derived base URL) and sends it verbatim to Cognito's `/logout`. Per AWS contract, if `logout_uri` is not in the app-client's Allowed sign-out URLs the user lands on a Cognito error page. A deployment behind a new hostname / LB alias / custom domain not registered leaves the user stranded after logout, with no platform-side recovery.
- proposed_doc_action: Add to the Cognito section a Known-limitations note: the post-logout redirect is derived from the hostname the user accessed; that hostname MUST be registered in the Cognito app-client's Allowed sign-out URLs; an unregistered hostname yields a Cognito error page after logout. Add every external hostname.
- cross_references: adjacent to the generic Host-header open-redirect caveat the wave shipped on the same page; no prior DOC-GAP covers the Cognito allowlist specifically.

### DOC-GAP-107 (was B02-F2)
- category: drift
- severity: LOW
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java auth logout:CognitoLogoutSuccessHandler (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[2] + bugs_limitations_corner_cases[1])
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/oauth2-oidc` 2026-05-29 status 200 — "NOT PRESENT" for any mention that Cognito's `/logout` does NOT sign users out of upstream federated / social IdPs. The wave's token-revocation matrix covers IdP-token-revocation but not the federated-signout nuance.
  - Code: `CognitoLogoutSuccessHandler.java:40-46` calls only Cognito's `/logout`; no upstream-IdP sign-out step. Per AWS docs, the logout endpoint doesn't sign users out of OIDC/social IdPs. A Cognito→Google deployment leaves the Google session active; next login skips the Google password prompt.
- proposed_doc_action: Add a one-line caveat under the Cognito section: when Cognito federates to an upstream IdP, ODD logout clears the ODD + Cognito sessions but NOT the upstream IdP session — the user's next login may skip the IdP password prompt. Cognito-platform behaviour, not configurable in ODD.
- cross_references: none (distinct from the token-revocation matrix; that is access-token revocation, this is federated-session signout).

### DOC-GAP-108 (was B02-F3)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java DataEntityAttachmentController controller-class:DataEntityAttachmentController (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings + no-quota/size); controller-method:uploadFileChunk (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[1])
- evidence:
  - PARALLEL-SURFACE CONTRADICTION between two live pages, both WebFetched 2026-05-29 status 200:
    - `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/attachments` STILL asserts (verbatim, re-confirmed twice): "Files larger than the cap are rejected by the upload API." (claims server-side enforcement).
    - `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` (attachment section) NOW states: "the server does not re-validate per-file size on the upload path ... there is no effective server-side total-file size limit."
  - Code confirms the config page is correct: `uploadFileChunk` reads no size (`DataEntityAttachmentController.java:54-62`), `FileServiceImpl.java:58-67` calls `transferTo` with no byte-count check; the cap is surfaced only as a UI hint via `getUploadOptions` (`AttachmentServiceImpl.java:60-62`). The feature page's "rejected by the upload API" claim is materially wrong (the statement DOC-GAP-005 was filed against); the wave fixed the config page but left the feature page's contradicting claim live.
- proposed_doc_action: Correct `features/data-discovery/attachments.md`: replace "Files larger than the cap are rejected by the upload API." with the accurate posture (cap is a UI-side filter only; chunked-upload API does not re-validate per-chunk/aggregate size; a non-browser caller with DATA_ENTITY_ATTACHMENT_MANAGE can submit arbitrarily-large files; for a hard cap use `spring.codec.max-in-memory-size` or a network-layer limit). Align with the corrected config page.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-005 (config-page side now CLOSED; this is the still-open feature-page side as a page-vs-page contradiction); REFACTOR-013 (HIGH) in `lineage/odd-platform/refactoring-scopes.md:107-115`.

### DOC-GAP-110 (was B03-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM

- evidence:

The wave ADDED 4 of the 5 DEG-membership facets (distinct ADD/DELETE permissions, empty-DEG persistence, activity-feed absence, silent-204-vs-400 asymmetry). The ONE facet NOT added: the HARD-delete-vs-soft-delete-cascade durability asymmetry (page does NOT mention it).
  - Code: `ReactiveGroupEntityRelationRepositoryImpl.java:57-65` — user-initiated removal issues `DSL.deleteFrom(GROUP_ENTITY_RELATIONS)` (HARD DELETE). `:92-112` — the entity-deletion cascade uses `softDeleteRelationsForDeletedDataEntities` (sets `IS_DELETED=true`) which `restoreRelationsForDataEntities` can resurrect. Net: a UI-removed membership never returns; an entity-deletion-removed membership DOES return on restore. `DataEntityServiceImpl.java:411-438` (user-DELETE uses the hard path).
- proposed_doc_action: Add one sentence to the groups-domains DEG-membership section: removing an entity from a group via the UI/API is a permanent (hard) delete; this is distinct from the automatic removal on entity-deletion, which is reversible (re-ingest restores prior memberships).
- cross_references: none (the 4 sibling facets are now CLOSED on the live page; this is the residual carve-out the wave missed).

### DOC-GAP-111 (was B03-F2)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java DataEntityController controller-method:createOwnership
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/owners` 2026-05-29 status 200. The wave largely closed DOC-GAP-087 (per-entity ownership-create flow + Title concept + Owner+Title auto-create). Residual: the page explicitly does NOT cover DEG-propagation of ownership ("What is NOT documented: DEG propagation of ownership to children entities; Duplicate-prevention error shapes").
  - Code: `OwnershipServiceImpl.java:62-67, 121-149` — when `formData.propagate == true` AND the target is a DEG, the new ownership row is cloned to EVERY child via `ownershipRepository.createOrUpdate(pojos)`; `ReactiveOwnershipRepositoryImpl.java:61-82` uses `.onConflictOnConstraint(OWNERSHIP_DATA_ENTITY_ID_OWNER_ID_KEY).doUpdate().set(OWNERSHIP.TITLE_ID, DSL.excluded(...))` — children that already have a row for this owner are silently RE-TITLED (cascade is title-overwriting, not skip-existing). No per-child activity event (`OwnershipServiceImpl.java:48` single `@ActivityLog` at the parent).
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "Propagating ownership to group members" note on the owners page: the `propagate` flag on POST /api/dataentities/{deg_id}/ownership cascades owner+title to every child; children that already have that owner are re-titled (not skipped); the cascade emits a single activity event on the parent DEG, not per child.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-087 (now largely CLOSED; this is the residual DEG-propagation facet).

### DOC-GAP-114 (was B05-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java DataSourceController controller-method:updateDataSource
- evidence: WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/management` 2026-05-29 status 200. The page documents the Datasources "add, edit, or remove" affordance but, asked about edit semantics, returns "EDITING a data source (REPLACE vs MERGE): Not described." Code (ede5d277): `PUT /api/datasources/{id}` is a full-form REPLACE — `DataSourceMapper.applyToPojo` (DataSourceMapper.java:49-56) is a MapStruct `@MappingTarget` and `MapperConfig` (MapperConfig.java:7-11) sets NO `nullValuePropertyMappingStrategy`, so MapStruct's default `SET_TO_NULL` governs; `DataSourceUpdateFormData` has 3 optional fields (`name`/`description`/`namespace_name`, components.yaml:1317-1325) with no `required` block — a body omitting `description` writes NULL over the existing description and omitting `namespace_name` nulls `namespace_id` (DataSourceMapper.java:55, detaching the namespace). No MERGE option; no live warning.
- proposed_doc_action: Add an "Editing replaces all fields" admonition to `features/management.md` (Datasources tab edit section): PUT /api/datasources/{id} is a full-form REPLACE, not a partial merge; the request must carry every field to keep (name, description, namespace_name); omitting a field nulls that column; the UI edit form resends all fields, programmatic callers must too; only those three fields are editable (ODDRN and collector token are not changed by an edit).
- cross_references: none (REPLACE-not-MERGE for data sources not in the prior set). Adjacent: the implicit-namespace-creation NAMESPACE_CREATE-bypass updateDataSource shares with registerDataSource is now CLOSED on management; this finding is scoped to the REPLACE-vs-MERGE data-loss gap only.

### DOC-GAP-122 (was B07-F2)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java org.opendatadiscovery.oddplatform.config config-class:SchedulingConfiguration (`bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0]` HIGH + `performance.known_performance_gaps.[0]` HIGH + `docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]`)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-29 status 200. The Housekeeping section mentions ShedLock + the session-housekeeping N×-redundancy, but is verbatim SILENT on: the single-thread scheduled executor, `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size`, and the consequence that all four @Scheduled jobs share one thread.
  - Code: no `TaskScheduler` @Bean anywhere and no `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size` in application.yml → Spring Boot default `ThreadPoolTaskScheduler` poolSize=1. The four @Scheduled consumers — HousekeepingJobManager (15m, lock 14m), DataEntityStatusSwitchJob (10m, lock 9m), PostgreSQLPartitionCreationJob (cron daily 00:01, lock 10m), PostgreSQLSessionHousekeepingJobHandler (1h, NO lock) — run sequentially on ONE thread. A 14-minute housekeeping cycle blocks the 10-minute status-switch; a cycle overrunning past midnight queues the daily partition-creation cron — and if next-day ACTIVITY/MESSAGE partitions are not CREATEd before first use, INSERTs fail with "no partition exists for the given key."
- proposed_doc_action: Add to the Housekeeping/scheduling section a note that ODD's scheduled jobs run on a single-thread executor by default (poolSize=1), so a long housekeeping cycle delays the other jobs and can push the daily 00:01 partition-creation cron late; recommend `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size: 4` for heavy-housekeeping deployments, and warn that a partition-creation cron delayed past a day boundary risks "no partition exists" INSERT failures.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-227 / DOC-GAP-148 / DOC-GAP-060 (sharded-registry/sibling scheduling gaps — session-housekeeping N×-redundancy now documented). The single-thread-executor / partition-cron-miss-midnight gap is NEW. LSN-019 (tunable-boundary class).

### DOC-GAP-123 (was B07-F3)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java OAuthSecurityConfiguration config-key-consumer:auth.type@L71 (`bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[6]` MEDIUM + `performance.known_performance_gaps.[0]` MEDIUM)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/oauth2-oidc` 2026-05-29 status 200. Targeted query: multi-replica / horizontal-scaling / sticky-sessions / shared-session-store / Redis / "authorization request not found" / in-memory WebSession default — ALL NOT MENTIONED. (The page now documents azure-tenant-id, logout-uri-required, the ODD_IAM logout matrix, and still lists Okta/Keycloak.)
  - Code: `OAuthSecurityConfiguration` declares no session-store bean — no @EnableSpringWebSession, no Redis/JDBC WebSessionStore (OAuthSecurityConfiguration.java:1-269). Spring Security WebFlux falls back to default in-memory WebSession. In a multi-replica deployment with no sticky sessions and no shared store, the OAuth2 authorization-request state is on whichever pod served `GET /oauth2/authorization/{regId}`; if the provider callback to `/login/oauth2/code/{regId}` is load-balanced to a different pod, login fails ("authorization_request_not_found"). The platform never states HA requires sticky sessions or a shared store.
- proposed_doc_action: Add an "HA / multi-replica deployments" admonition to `oauth2-oidc.md` (and/or the parent authentication page): OAuth2/OIDC login stores the in-flight authorization request in an in-memory session by default; in multi-replica you MUST configure sticky sessions OR a shared session store, else the callback can land on a different pod and login fails with "authorization request not found." ODD ships no shared-session-store bean — operator-side deployment prerequisite.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-221 / DOC-GAP-175 (sharded-registry session-lifecycle / logout-asymmetry siblings — different concerns). The OAuth2 HA session-store gap is NEW.

### DOC-GAP-127 (was B09-F3)

High-throughput sizing recommendations are completely absent").
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/master-data-management/lookup-tables` 2026-05-29 status 200 — same: "R2DBC pool-sizing guidance is explicitly absent."
  - Code (ede5d277): `R2DBCConfiguration.java:41-50` + `:75-84` wire a `PropertyMapper.alwaysApplyingWhenNonNull()` over ten `R2dbcProperties.Pool` parameters. `application.yml` ships zero `spring.r2dbc.pool.*` keys → every value at Spring Boot framework default (`r2dbc-pool 1.0.0.RELEASE` → maxSize=10 per pool). Two pools are ALWAYS instantiated (`customConnectionPool` has no @ConditionalOnProperty at `:54`), so per-replica ceiling is 2 × 10 = 20 connections; a 5-replica deployment can pin ~100 PG connection slots (PG default max_connections=100). The concept catalog names this as the saturation bound for the authorization hot path.
- proposed_doc_action: Add an "R2DBC connection-pool sizing" subsection to `configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform.md` documenting at minimum `spring.r2dbc.pool.max-size` (and the framework default of 10/pool), and the load-bearing fact that two R2DBC pools are opened per replica regardless of whether `spring.custom-datasource.*` is set — so the effective per-replica PG-connection budget is 2 × max-size. Cross-link the lookup-tables page.
- cross_references: none (no prior DOC-GAP covers R2DBC pool sizing; DOC-163 F-059 is metrics.storage, unrelated).

### DOC-GAP-129 (was B10-F1)
- category: missing-page
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java repository reactive repository:ReactiveCollectorRepositoryImpl (corroborated by ReactiveDataSourceRepositoryImpl)
- evidence:
  - The collector/datasource bearer token (`TOKEN.value`) is the single credential a collector presents on every `POST /ingestion/datasources` call. The Collector-repo sidecar (ede5d277) records it is stored as PLAINTEXT at rest: no hashing, no UNIQUE constraint on `TOKEN.value` (cross-collector sharing race), plaintext-equality comparison, present in clear text in `pg_dump`, backups, and read-replicas. The DataSource-repo `getByToken` read path confirms plaintext lookup at the SQL tier.
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/s2s` 2026-05-29 status 200 — discusses the per-collector ingestion bearer token but is ABSENT on at-rest storage ("no warning ... that collector/datasource token values are stored as plaintext in the Postgres TOKEN table, nor any caveat about hashing, uniqueness, backups, or database dumps").
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/developer-guides/build-and-run/custom-collectors` 2026-05-29 status 200 — ABSENT ("does not discuss token storage, hashing, or security implications at the database layer").
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "Token storage and at-rest exposure" admonition to the S2S page (cross-link from build-and-run/custom-collectors): the collector/datasource ingestion token is stored as plain text in the TOKEN table — NOT hashed, no UNIQUE constraint, so it appears in pg_dump, base backups, and any read-replica. Protect the database/volume at rest (disk encryption), restrict pg_dump and replica access, treat a leaked backup as full collector-credential compromise. Note rotation issues a new plaintext value and does not retro-protect old backups.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-034 (Token Rotation operational mechanics / plaintext-in-RESPONSE — adjacent but distinct: that is response-time exposure; THIS is at-rest storage). NOT covered by DOC-GAP-006 / DOC-GAP-050. No prior DOC-GAP covers the ingestion TOKEN.value at-rest plaintext shape.

### DOC-GAP-130 (was B10-F2)

But it remains ABSENT on the duplicate-ownership error contract ("no documentation of error codes, HTTP 400 responses, or the specific error 'USR003' / 'Ownership for this data entity and owner already exists'").
  - Ownership-repo sidecar (ede5d277): a duplicate `POST /api/dataentities/{id}/ownership` produces HTTP 400 with ErrorCode `UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT` (`USR003`, resolvable=true, retryable=false — `ErrorCode.java:11`) and message "Ownership for this data entity and owner already exists" (`ExceptionUtils.java:69-71`). Path: jOOQ DataAccessException → `JooqReactiveOperations.mono(query).onErrorMap(...)` (:41) → `ExceptionUtils.translateDatabaseException` matches `C23_INTEGRITY_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATION` (:30-36) → `UniqueConstraintException` → `ControllerAdvice.handleUniqueConstraint` maps to BAD_REQUEST (:36-40). The live OpenAPI does not declare this 400 either.
- proposed_doc_action: Add the duplicate-ownership error contract to the Owners page API section (and/or the OpenAPI responses): re-creating an existing (data_entity, owner) ownership returns HTTP 400 with code USR003 and the message above — a resolvable, non-retryable client error, NOT a 5xx. Consumers should treat 400/USR003 as "already exists" and not retry.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-075 (Owners page omissions — POST/OWNER_CREATE/audit now CLOSED; this error-contract sub-finding survives). DOC-GAP-074 (201-vs-200 on createOwner — separate concern). See also DOC-GAP-135 (substrate correction: a prior sidecar mis-recorded this as 5xx).

- category: drift

    - (a) Soft-delete-name-reuse recreation: deleting a policy named X then creating a new X yields a NEW id with EMPTY role bindings; the old id's `role_to_policy` edges/historical references are NOT remapped. Partial unique index is `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (`ReactivePolicyRepositoryImpl.java:19` + `ReactiveAbstractSoftDeleteCRUDRepository.java:50-59` + `V0_0_55__add_policies_and_roles.sql:30`).
    - (b) Ghost-permission via direct DB manipulation: no FK cascade between `policy.deleted_at` and `role_to_policy.policy_id`. The custom `getRolesPolicies` (:32-38) does NOT filter `deleted_at IS NULL`, so a policy soft-deleted by a direct DB UPDATE (bypassing `PolicyServiceImpl.delete`'s CascadeDeleteException guard at `PolicyServiceImpl.java:89-92`) leaves surviving edges that still grant permissions.
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "Deleting and re-creating policies" note to the Policies page: policy deletion is a soft-delete; the name becomes immediately reusable but a re-created policy is a fresh id with NO inherited role bindings. Warn against manipulating `policy.deleted_at` directly in the DB — the service-layer cascade guard is bypassed by raw SQL, producing "ghost" policies whose `role_to_policy` edges still grant. Fold into the same "Who can create / mutate policies" H2 proposed for DOC-GAP-073.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-073 (Policies page omits POLICY_CREATE / Administrator-seed / audit-absence / GET /api/policies/schema / DISABLED-bypass — re-confirmed STILL OPEN 2026-05-29). DOC-GAP-083 META. This soft-delete-recreation + ghost-permission pair is NOT among the DOC-GAP-073 five. See DOC-GAP-165 (UI-soft-delete-still-grants — the normal-delete-path manifestation, related but distinct from the raw-SQL ghost path here).

CLOSES feature-existence, type-semantics, case-sensitivity drift.

Code: partial indexes at `V0_0_1__init.sql:238-244`: INTERNAL uses NAME-only key (so `cost_centre:STRING` blocks a second INTERNAL `cost_centre:INTEGER`) while EXTERNAL uses (TYPE, NAME) (a collector emitting both types creates two rows). An operator creating a field via the UI cannot predict the collision.
- proposed_doc_action: Add one paragraph to `features/data-discovery/custom-metadata`: INTERNAL (operator-curated) fields are unique by NAME alone — you cannot have two internal fields with the same name but different types; the second create silently resolves to the existing field. EXTERNAL (collector-ingested) fields are unique by (type, name), so a collector emitting cost_centre as both STRING and INTEGER produces two separate rows.
- cross_references: none (no prior DOC-GAP covers the custom-metadata uniqueness model; the broader "no custom-metadata page" gap is now CLOSED by the NEW page).

### DOC-GAP-136 (was B11-F1)

It does NOT disclose that highlightDataEntity interpolates the query into raw SQL (a code-execution-class issue). The autocomplete/suggestions surface is also undocumented (see DOC-GAP-137).
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/developer-guides/api-reference/search` 2026-05-29 status 404 — no api-reference page for the search surface.

Doc-side until fixed: upgrade the search page's query-syntax caveat from "avoid these characters or you get a 500" to a security admonition naming the highlight endpoint as a raw-SQL-interpolation surface (operators with auth.type=DISABLED, or any multi-tenant deployment, are exposed to arbitrary read-side SQL via a crafted search query that later flows through highlight). Distinguish from the tsquery-DoS caveat already shipped.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-080 (tsquery DoS — CLOSED by the wave's special-char warning; THIS is a distinct, more severe raw-`.formatted()` SQL-injection the closure does NOT cover). DOC-GAP-096 (markdown/rehype stored-XSS — different class). No prior DOC-GAP covers getHighlightedResult raw-SQL interpolation. Candidate /log-issue odd-platform.

### DOC-GAP-140 (was B11-F5; merged with B13-F4)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM

- evidence:
  - Term-repo sidecar (ede5d277): `hasDescriptionRelations(termId)` (`ReactiveTermRepositoryImpl.java:409-438`) is the guard `TermServiceImpl.updateTerm` (rename-protection, :128) and `TermServiceImpl.delete` (delete-protection, :156) consult — it EXISTS-checks the three link tables filtered to `is_description_link = TRUE` AND parent `data_entity.status != DELETED` (:415, :425). Operator-visible: a term mentioned in any ACTIVE description cannot be renamed/deleted (400 BadUserRequestException "Can't update/delete term, which was mentioned in description"). Corner case the guard's `STATUS != DELETED` predicate creates: a term mentioned ONLY in a soft-deleted entity's description PASSES the guard (returns FALSE) → the term can be hard-deleted/renamed → when the entity is later restored to ACTIVE, its `[[ns:term]]` text dangles against a non-existent/renamed term and silently downgrades to "unhandled" on the next description edit.
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-glossary/business-glossary` 2026-05-29 status 200 — the wave now documents the auto-link side-channel + the DATA_ENTITY_ADD_TERM bypass (those CLOSED), but does NOT document (a) the rename/delete-protection guard at all ("the page does not mention any 400-error protection when renaming or deleting terms referenced in active descriptions"), nor (b) the soft-deleted-entity dangling-term corner case.
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-glossary` 2026-05-29 status 200 — parent page, silent on the guard.
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "Renaming and deleting terms" subsection to `features/data-glossary/business-glossary`: a term mentioned in any active entity/column description (via `[[ns:term]]`) cannot be renamed or deleted — the platform returns a 400; remove the mentions first; edit the definition freely. Add the corner-case admonition: the protection EXCLUDES descriptions on soft-deleted (status=DELETED) entities, so a term mentioned only in a deleted entity's description CAN be removed; restoring that entity afterward leaves a dangling mention that silently becomes "unhandled".
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-100 ([[ns:term]] syntax — business-glossary page now EXISTS and documents the auto-link side-channel, but the exact `[[namespace:term]]` syntax is still shown only in figures, not spelled out in text — DOC-GAP-100 PARTIALLY closed). No prior DOC-GAP covers the rename/delete-protection guard or the dangling-term-on-restore corner case. (Merged dedup: frag-13 B13-F4 filed the same guard finding from the TermServiceImpl/TermController tier; evidence folded here.)

### DOC-GAP-143 (was B12-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java service service:IngestionServiceImpl (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[0,1]) + service service:IngestionService (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[0])
- evidence:
  - The wave PARTIALLY closed DOC-GAP-092 (was "POST /ingestion/entities doc-orphaned, no page"): WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/developer-guides/api-reference` 2026-05-29 status 200 NOW names the endpoint ("POST /ingestion/entities is the load-bearing call every collector makes for each metadata batch. The endpoint returns HTTP 200 on success."). The endpoint is no longer page-orphaned. BUT the SERVICE-LAYER DESTRUCTIVE SEMANTICS remain undocumented on every page that names it.
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/s2s` 2026-05-29 status 200 — the only page with a POST /ingestion/entities example: "no transaction semantics guarantees; no warnings about silent metadata or lineage destruction; no mention of replace-not-merge behavior or batch atomicity."
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/integrations/integrations/ingestion-filters` 2026-05-29 status 200 — covers collector-side regex include/exclude only ("does not discuss API endpoints, authentication configuration, transaction semantics, metadata handling strategies, or search indexing operations").
  - Code (IngestionServiceImpl @ ede5d277): the 14-processor pipeline runs as a SINGLE @ReactiveTransactional per batch — one bad entity rolls back the ENTIRE batch (no per-entity isolation, no DLQ); lineage and metadata writes are REPLACE-NOT-MERGE (a re-POST omitting a previously-sent edge/field silently DELETEs it); soft-deleted entities are silently restored on re-ingest; datasource scoping is payload-driven with no principal check.
- proposed_doc_action: When the canonical operator-facing ingestion page is authored (the DOC-GAP-092 follow-up), add an "Ingestion semantics" section documenting: (1) batch atomicity — all-or-nothing; if any entity fails, NONE are written; (2) replace-not-merge — lineage edges + metadata are REPLACED per POST, not merged; (3) soft-delete restore-on-reingest; (4) no dead-letter queue. Cross-link from the api-reference landing page's POST /ingestion/entities line and the s2s example.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-092 (page-existence half now PARTIALLY closed; the destructive-semantics half is the surviving gap). F-008 (single_transaction_per_batch + silent_destruction_replace_not_merge). Adjacent to DOC-GAP-118 (datasource re-registration merge — that is /ingestion/datasources; THIS is /ingestion/entities).

- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java service service:HousekeepingJobManager (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[1] — ShedLock window)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` (#housekeeping) 2026-05-29 status 200 — the wave rewrote this section well (5 cleanup tasks, Java-default-0 wipe, jOOQ-bug-with-workaround). It states "fires every 15 minutes" + "Guarded by ShedLock" but ABSENT: lockAtMostFor window specification, per-job failure isolation details.
  - Code (HousekeepingJobManager.java:26 @ ede5d277): `lockAtLeastFor = "14m"` AND `lockAtMostFor = "14m"`, against `@Scheduled(fixedRate = 15, timeUnit = MINUTES)` (line 25). Only 60s of slack. A cycle that runs ≥14 minutes (a large DataEntityHousekeepingJob ~25-table cascade holding one shared JDBC connection for all 5 jobs sequentially — line 32 acquires ONE connection per cycle) can release the lock at the 14-minute mark while still committing, allowing a SECOND instance to acquire at the 15-minute mark and run concurrently. The docs say "ShedLock for multi-instance coordination" with no values.
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "Multi-instance deployment" note to the housekeeping section: the ShedLock holds for lockAtMostFor=14m against a 15-minute schedule (~60s slack). On large databases the lock may release before the cycle finishes, letting a second replica start the next cycle concurrently. Operators running N>1 replicas with large catalogs should keep cascade volumes bounded (tune TTLs) or be aware concurrent housekeeping runs are possible at the schedule boundary. State the 14m/15m values explicitly.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-059 (Java-default-0 wipe — CLOSED) + DOC-GAP-060 (3-vs-5 jobs — CLOSED). This ShedLock-window caveat is NOT among either and is NOT in the prior set.

### DOC-GAP-146 (was B12-F4)
- category: drift
- severity: LOW
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java service service:HousekeepingJobManager (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[2] — per-job failure isolation)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` (#housekeeping) 2026-05-29 status 200 — ABSENT for per-job failure-isolation details. The section describes the cleanup tasks as if all run to completion; it does not document what happens when ONE job throws.
  - Code (HousekeepingJobManager.java:41-47 @ ede5d277): `runHousekeepingJob` wraps each job in an inner try/catch that logs `log.error("Error while running a housekeeping job", e)` and CONTINUES. One failing job does NOT stall the others — but there is NO structured audit, NO metric, NO health-check signal; the only failure surface is an ERROR log line with a generic message (the failing job is not even named in the log).
- proposed_doc_action: Add one line to the housekeeping section: each cleanup job runs independently within the 15-minute cycle; if one fails, it is logged at ERROR level and the remaining jobs still run. There is no metric or health-check for housekeeping failures today — operators needing alerting should match the ERROR-level log line. Pair with DOC-GAP-145 in the same "Operational behaviour" note.
- cross_references: none directly; complements DOC-GAP-145 (same page/source/theme). Adjacent to the no-audit/observability theme in DOC-GAP-055 + DOC-GAP-043 (failure-swallowed + no operator signal, different subsystem).

### DOC-GAP-147 (was B12-F5)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java service service:PolicyServiceImpl (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings SERVICE-DOC-GAP-A + B + D)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/policies` 2026-05-29 status 200 — the wave did NOT touch this page for these concerns. ABSENT for ALL of: POLICY_CREATE, transactionality/lost-update, cascade-delete behavior, Administrator-name asymmetry, malformed-body 500-vs-400, GET /api/policies/schema, DISABLED-bypass. The page still "focuses on policy JSON structure, condition operators, and performance characteristics."
  - Code (PolicyServiceImpl @ ede5d277), three operator-visible service-tier behaviours:
    - (a) Lost-update race: `update` reads `get(id)` (line 74) then `updateOne` (line 79) with NO @ReactiveTransactional spanning the two — two concurrent PUT /api/policies/{id} can lost-update silently. The sibling `RoleServiceImpl.update` IS transactional, so the asymmetry is a quirk.
    - (b) Cascade-delete error contract: `delete` checks `isPolicyAttachedToRole(id)` (line 89) and throws `CascadeDeleteException("Policy is attached to a role")` (4xx) if still bound to any role. Operators scripting cleanup hit this; the fix is dependency ordering.
    - (c) Malformed-body 500-not-400: schema-validation failure raises IllegalArgumentException → `ControllerAdvice.handleServerException` (:61-66) → HTTP 500 "Internal Server Error", NOT a 400 with validator detail.
- proposed_doc_action: Fold into the same "Who can create / mutate policies" H2 proposed for DOC-GAP-073, adding a "Policy mutation semantics" sub-section: (1) PUT /api/policies/{id} is NOT transactional — concurrent edits can lose updates with no error; serialise policy edits; (2) deleting a policy still attached to a role returns a 4xx "Policy is attached to a role" — detach from all roles first; (3) a malformed policy body returns HTTP 500 (not 400) — validate against GET /api/policies/schema before submitting.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-073 (Policies page omits POLICY_CREATE + Administrator-bootstrap + audit + schema endpoint + DISABLED-bypass — re-confirmed STILL ABSENT; these three transactional-semantics sub-findings are NOT among the DOC-GAP-073 five). DOC-GAP-131 (Policies soft-delete-recreation + ghost-permission — also for this page; distinct). DOC-GAP-083 META.

### DOC-GAP-148 (was B12-F6)
- category: drift
- severity: LOW
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java service service:OwnershipServiceImpl (docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[4] — self-grant ambiguity; [1] — Title directory)
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions` 2026-05-29 status 200 — "does not address whether a user can grant themselves ownership or whether DATA_ENTITY_OWNERSHIP_CREATE binds the owner to the caller's principal." Also "No TITLE_CREATE permission exists ... the mechanism by which titles enter the directory is not documented in this permissions page."
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/policies` 2026-05-29 status 200 — ABSENT for any example Policy granting DATA_ENTITY_OWNERSHIP_CREATE and ABSENT for the list of default shipped Policies; an operator cannot determine whether a fresh install lets regular users self-grant ownership.
  - Code (OwnershipServiceImpl @ 80637ed/ede5d277): `create` reads `formData.getOwnerName()` VERBATIM (line 52) — does NOT infer the owner from the caller's principal. A Policy granting DATA_ENTITY_OWNERSHIP_CREATE unconditionally lets the holder grant ANY owner (including a newly-minted one via auto-create-on-miss) to ANY entity — including self-grant. Separately, `titleService.getOrCreate(formData.getTitleName())` (line 53) auto-creates Title rows from caller-supplied free-text with no TITLE_CREATE permission (grep TITLE_CREATE across odd-platform-api returns zero matches).
- proposed_doc_action: On the Permissions page, add to the DATA_ENTITY_OWNERSHIP_CREATE entry: this permission does NOT bind the owner to the caller's principal — a holder can assign any owner (existing or newly-minted) to any data entity, including self-grant. On the Policies page, enumerate the default shipped Policies and state whether regular users can self-grant ownership out of the box. Document that ownership titles are caller-supplied free-text auto-created on first use (no Title allowlist, no TITLE_CREATE permission).
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-087 (Ownership-create + Owner+Title auto-create bypass — the OWNER auto-create half is now CLOSED on the owners page; the TITLE half + self-grant binding nuance survive and are this finding). DOC-GAP-073/DOC-GAP-075 (RBAC pages omit semantics — adjacent). Distinct from DOC-GAP-130 (owner USR003 error contract).

### DOC-GAP-149 (was B13-F1; merged with B14-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java TagController controller-method:deleteTag (+ java service:TagServiceImpl bugs_limitations_corner_cases[asymmetric-cascade]); also odd-platform openapi tags openapi-tag:tag (`doc_drift_findings[3]` deleteTag cascade) — the same gap filed independently as B14-F1, merged here
- evidence:
  - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/tagging` 2026-05-29 status 200 — the page documents the LSN-019 popular-ordering drift AND the tag side-door AND the `TAG_DELETE` permission, BUT on tag DELETE cascade it is silent: "Not documented ... no detail on whether deletion cascades to entities, terms, or column assignments."
  - Code: `TagServiceImpl.delete` (TagServiceImpl.java:57-70, @ReactiveTransactional) HARD-deletes `tag_to_term` + `tag_to_data_entity` (Flux.zip at :64-65), SOFT-deletes the `tag` row (UPDATE deleted_at), and NEVER calls `deleteDatasetFieldRelations(long)` (ReactiveTagRepositoryImpl.java:299-306 — defined but uncalled). `tag_to_dataset_field` (per-column INTERNAL-origin assignments) rows survive as orphans referencing a soft-deleted tag id; no reaper job purges them.
  - Second-order: the `!external` delete guard reads only `boolOr(tag_to_data_entity.external)` (getDto, ReactiveTagRepositoryImpl.java:54-66) — a tag whose only Collector-set origin is an EXTERNAL `tag_to_dataset_field.origin` row passes the guard and is deletable by a UI user, silently orphaning a Collector-owned dataset-field tag link.
  - Corroborated by feature-flow F-018 (delete_tag_cascade_asymmetric_tag_to_dataset_field_rows_orphaned) and F-047 (tag_to_dataset_field INTERNAL channel, delete-all-then-recreate) [B14-F1 merge].
- proposed_doc_action: Add a "What happens when you delete a tag" note to the tagging page: tag row is soft-deleted (recoverable, invisible to UI); data-entity + term links are removed; dataset-column (dataset-field) tag links are NOT removed and persist as orphans; a Collector-set (external) tag cannot be deleted — but the external check only covers data-entity relations, so a tag external only on dataset fields can be deleted. (If the orphan behaviour is itself a defect, also log an upstream code issue.)
- cross_references: F-018 / F-047 (feature-flows, same polarity, STRENGTHEN); P-032/P-033 (code-side probes). No prior DOC-GAP-NN (new). (Merged dedup: frag-14 B14-F1 filed the same tag-delete cascade asymmetry from the openapi-tag sidecar; evidence folded here.)

### DOC-GAP-152 (was B13-F5)
- category: drift
- severity: LOW
- surfaced_by: odd-platform java TagController controller-method:updateTag (bugs_limitations_corner_cases[0])
- evidence:
  - Code: `TagServiceImpl.update` ends with `.map(tagMapper::mapToTag)` on a bare `TagPojo` (TagServiceImpl.java:54); the `mapToTag(TagPojo)` overload (TagMapper.java:26) maps only id, name, important. The OpenAPI `Tag` schema declares `external` + `usedCount`, and the `mapToTag(TagDto)` overload (TagMapper.java:23-24) — used by getPopularTagList — DOES populate them. So `PUT /api/tags/{id}` returns a Tag with external/usedCount null/absent, inconsistent with the GET /api/tags items. A UI re-rendering a tag chip from the update response loses the usage count + external flag.
  - Doc context: the api-reference index (2026-05-29, 200) has no tags sub-page and the OpenAPI spec is the only contract; the spec declares the full Tag shape for the update response, so this is a spec-vs-implementation response-shape drift, not a doc-prose gap.
- proposed_doc_action: Code fix preferred (swap to the `mapToTag(TagDto)` overload, or re-fetch the DTO post-update). If documenting instead: note in the OpenAPI updateTag response (and any future tags api-reference page) that external/usedCount are not populated on the update response — clients must re-read via GET /api/tags.
- cross_references: none (distinct from the 201-vs-200 status drift class DOC-GAP-074; this is a response-body field-completeness drift).

Only the "Add query example" button is gated, via the separate `<WithPermissions>` at `QueryExamples.tsx:36-46`. A user WITHOUT QUERY_EXAMPLE_CREATE still sees the full list (search box, items, count) and only loses the Add button.
  - Live page confirms ABSENT: no clarification that a user lacking the permission still reads the list; no statement that the Relationships surface is ungated/readable by any authenticated user.
- proposed_doc_action: Correct the `features/data-modelling.md` RBAC line — QUERY_EXAMPLE_CREATE gates only the "Add query example" action (and _UPDATE/_DELETE the per-example edit/delete); the Query Examples LIST and the Relationships surface are read-open to any authenticated user. Reword to "Query Examples list (read-open); creation requires QUERY_EXAMPLE_CREATE" or add the explicit read-vs-write split.
- cross_references: same read-collaborative class as DOC-GAP-082 / read-collaborative META cluster, but a distinct INCORRECT-claim instance on /data-modelling (Priority-1 actively-misleading: doc overstates a restriction the code does not enforce). NOT in prior set → NEW. (Substantively CLOSED on the child query-examples page per DOC-GAP-171/frag-18.)

### DOC-GAP-162 (was B16-F1)
- category: drift
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform ts react-component component:RolesList
- evidence: WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/roles` (2026-05-30, status 200) — the page names the predefined roles as `USER` and `ADMIN` (uppercase): "USER — a regular user with no permissions by default. ADMIN — an administrator with every permission." But the platform's UserProviderRole enum and the UI guard use mixed-case literals: `RoleItem.tsx:24-25` checks `name === 'Administrator'` and `name === 'User'` (case-SENSITIVE); `RoleForm.tsx:28` disables the name field on `name === 'User'`. Docs show ADMIN/USER; code reserves Administrator/User. Load-bearing because UI Edit/Delete-button suppression hinges on the exact mixed-case string (a role named `administrator` lowercase would NOT be matched and WOULD show the buttons).
- proposed_doc_action: On /authorization/roles, render the predefined role names exactly as seeded (`Administrator` / `User`, per `V0_0_56__add_predefined_roles_and_policies.sql`) rather than ADMIN/USER; add a one-line note that the reservation guard is case-sensitive so a different-case clone is not treated as predefined.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-072 (Roles page omits role-creation API surface incl. predefined-name reservation asymmetry) — adjacent but distinct; DOC-GAP-072 covers the creation/reservation asymmetry, this covers the spelling mismatch of the predefined names. Not a re-file.

### DOC-GAP-163 (was B16-F2)
- category: missing-page
- severity: MEDIUM

- evidence: WebFetch `/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/roles` (2026-05-30, status 200) — "the documentation does not describe differential visibility of roles based on caller permissions" (NOT PRESENT). Code: `RoleServiceImpl.list` (RoleServiceImpl.java:40-47) forks — ADMIN callers get the full paginated catalog via `roleRepository.listDto`, non-ADMIN callers get ONLY their own attached roles via in-memory `filterUserRoles`. `RolesList.tsx:126-128` renders whatever the API returns with NO "showing N of M" affordance. A non-ADMIN operator opening Management→Roles sees one row (their own) and would conclude the platform has one role configured.

(An inversion of the platform's usual read-collaborative posture — worth calling out.)

### DOC-GAP-165 (was B16-F4)
- category: missing-page
- severity: HIGH
- surfaced_by: odd-platform ts react-component component:PolicyList
- evidence: WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/policies` (2026-05-30, status 200) — soft-deleted-policy-still-grants warning NOT PRESENT; the page covers only JSON policy structure + condition operators. Code: the Policies LIST (`PolicyServiceImpl.list` → `policyRepository.list`) auto-applies `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (soft-delete base class), so a deleted policy DISAPPEARS from the tab; but the GRANT path `getRolesPolicies` (ReactivePolicyRepositoryImpl.java:32-38) is NOT soft-delete-filtered, so a role still bound to a soft-deleted policy CONTINUES to confer that policy's permissions on every authorized request. An operator who "deletes" a policy via the UI sees it gone but it keeps granting — a security-relevant catalogue-vs-grant asymmetry (LSN-001-shape silent no-op).
- proposed_doc_action: Add a danger admonition to /authorization/policies: "Soft-deleting a policy removes it from the Policies tab but does NOT revoke it — any role still bound to it keeps granting its permissions until you remove the binding on the Roles tab. To fully revoke, first detach it from every role, then delete it." Mirror a one-liner on /authorization/roles.
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-073 (Policies page omits POLICY_CREATE / audit / DISABLED-bypass) — its 5 sub-findings do NOT include the soft-delete-still-grants asymmetry; this is a new HIGH sub-finding on the same page. Same drift_class as F-006 soft_delete_aware_visibility_inconsistency. Related to DOC-GAP-131 (the raw-SQL ghost-permission path; this is the normal-UI-delete path — distinct mechanisms).

### DOC-GAP-166 (was B16-F5)
- category: missing-page
- severity: MEDIUM
- surfaced_by: odd-platform ts react-component component:OwnersList
- evidence: WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/owners` (2026-05-30, status 200) — the page now documents the empty-roles destructive UPDATE + the audit-silence, BUT the soft-deleted-owner visibility asymmetry is NOT PRESENT. Code (per F-019 deleteOwner sidecar + OwnersList invariants): the Owners LIST hides soft-deleted owners (`ReactiveOwnerRepositoryImpl.list` adds `deleted_at IS NULL`), but `GET /api/owners/{id}` returns soft-deleted rows. An operator who soft-deletes an Owner sees it gone from the directory yet a by-id API read (and any cached reference) still resolves it; `owner.name` is a NON-partial UNIQUE so the name stays reserved.
- proposed_doc_action: Add to /authorization/owners: "The Owners tab shows only active owners. A soft-deleted owner is hidden from the list but still resolvable via GET /api/owners/{id}, and its name remains reserved (the owner.name unique index is not partial), so the name cannot be reused until a DBA hard-deletes the row or applies a partial-unique migration."
- cross_references: DOC-GAP-075 (Owners page omits creation mechanics + soft-delete recovery semantics) — its sub-finding (6) covers "soft-delete recovery — name permanently held"; the by-id-visibility asymmetry is adjacent but not identical (visibility vs name-reuse). Strengthens DOC-GAP-075; recommend folding as a 7th sub-finding rather than a separate DOC-NNN.

Batch F adds **two NEW meta-findings** (DOC-GAP-094 doc-vs-code spelling/format mismatch; DOC-GAP-095 read-collaborative 4-sidecar) and strengthens five existing patterns:
  - (i) **NEW batch F: Read-collaborative cross-owner enumeration — now 4-sidecar (DOC-GAP-095 META)** — strengthened by `DataEntityController.getDataEntityDetails`, joining `getAllAlerts` (batch A), `getActivity` (batch B), `SearchController.search` (batch E). DataEntityDetails is **the centerpiece UI read of the platform** — every entity-detail page mount, every tab-switch, every browser refresh. A single ID-enumeration loop against `GET /api/dataentities/{1..N}` yields the complete catalog (entity classes, types, data sources, soft-delete state) with full 34-field payload (descriptions, ownership, metadata, terms, tags, lineage shortcuts, source URLs, view counts). The blast radius is wider than alerts/activity/search COMBINED. Live `/features/data-discovery` 200 + `/features/data-discovery/catalog-overview` 200 + `/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization` 200 all silent on read-endpoint posture (re-verified 2026-05-12 batch F). **STRONGEST single piece of evidence resolving ADR-CANDIDATE-003 borderline** — the silence is consistent across 4 distinct first-class platform surfaces AND across 3 dedicated security pages. The maintainer's call narrows to: (a) treat the silence AS the design intent and author a unified "Read-collaborative posture" section on the Authorization parent page documenting that any authenticated user reads any entity's full details, OR (b) treat the silence as a missed gate cluster and add per-feature permission gates. Either resolves the drift; doc-product silence on the centerpiece read is the decisive signal that one resolution is required.
  - (j) **NEW batch F: Doc-vs-code spelling/format mismatch — now 2-sidecar (DOC-GAP-094 META)** — `username-attribute` (descriptive prose) vs `user-name-attribute` (every YAML example) on `oauth2-oidc.md` (DOC-GAP-063 batch D) + `X-API-Key` header (curl example on `s2s.md`) vs `Authorization: Bearer` header (consumed by `IngestionDataEntitiesFilter` when `auth.ingestion.filter.enabled=true`) (DOC-GAP-091 batch F). Pattern: docs use one format/spelling while code expects another; operators copy-pasting docs verbatim get silent binding failure or 401. Recommend a doc-side spelling/format audit + a code-side alias mechanism (`@Name` annotations / multi-header acceptance) per affected surface.
  - (k) **STRENGTHENED batch F: Activity-feed retention claim — now 3-angle confirmed (DOC-GAP-041)** — joins existing partition-manager (WIDTH only, batch B) + HousekeepingTTLProperties (no activity scope, batch D) with `DataEntityController.updateStatus` mapper bug (DOC-GAP-088 batch F): `DataEntityMapperImpl.applyStatus` sets `pojo.setStatus(...)` BEFORE the `if (statusDto.getId() != pojo.getStatus())` check, so `statusUpdatedAt` is NEVER set on any transition. The 30-day `data_entity_delete_days` TTL relies on `STATUS_UPDATED_AT.lessOrEqual(now - N days)`; with the column always NULL, the predicate evaluates to NULL (≈ false in SQL three-valued logic). Soft-deleted entities never hard-delete. **Third independent angle confirming the platform has no functioning row-by-age retention path** — this is now the strongest LSN-001-class case in the catalog.
  - (l) **STRENGTHENED batch F: DISABLED-bypasses-everything (DOC-GAP-082 META extended)** — the 8-sidecar RBAC triangulation extends in batch F to: (m1) `DataEntityController.getDataEntityDetails` — DISABLED-mode anonymous read of the entire catalog's detail metadata (HIGH per sidecar); (m2) `DataEntityController.getDataEntityDownstreamLineage` — DISABLED-mode anonymous graph-shaped cross-owner enumeration (HIGH per sidecar); (m3) `DataEntityController.updateStatus` — DISABLED-mode anonymous wholesale data-entity soft-deletion (HIGH per sidecar); (m4) `DataEntityController.createOwnership` — DISABLED-mode anonymous ownership-binding establishment with auto-create Owner+Title side effects (HIGH per sidecar); (m5) `IngestionController.postDataEntityList` — already known via DOC-GAP-038 ingestion-filter-OFF default. The DISABLED-bypass META now covers **13 sidecars** across the RBAC primary surface + DataEntity read/write surface + ingestion surface. The pattern: spec declares 201 Created, implementation returns 200 OK via `ResponseEntity::ok` or `ResponseEntity.ok().build()`. Sibling `postDataSetStatsList` and `ingestMetrics` correctly return 201 — postDataEntityList is the only inconsistent endpoint in IngestionController, suggesting an unintentional drift rather than a deliberate upsert-vs-insert distinction. Class-wide audit recommended.

Batch E-and-prior meta-recommendations (preserved):
  - (e) **NEW batch E: DISABLED-bypasses-RBAC-primary-surface (DOC-GAP-082 META — 8-sidecar triangulation)** — `auth.type=DISABLED` (the application.yml-bundled default per DOC-GAP-036) silently bypasses the entire RBAC primary surface (POST /api/policies, POST /api/roles, POST /api/owners — all NO_CONTEXT MANAGEMENT-tier gates) because `DisabledAuthSecurityConfiguration.java:13-18` calls `.anyExchange().permitAll()` and never wires `AuthorizationCustomizer`. Live `/disabled-authentication` 200 contains only the generic "DO NOT use this method in your production environment!" warning; it does NOT enumerate that DISABLED mode means any network-reachable caller can create MANAGEMENT/ALL policies, attach them to arbitrary roles, attach those to owners — the full keys-to-the-kingdom escalation chain is open and silent. Live `/authorization` 200 + `/authorization/policies` 200 + `/authorization/roles` 200 + `/authorization/owners` 200 + `/authorization/permissions` 200 all silent on DISABLED-mode bypass. Activity feed (`/api/activity` — Activity Feed concept) does NOT cover RBAC mutations (grep verified: `@ActivityLog` appears only on AlertHaltConfigServiceImpl / AlertServiceImpl / DataEntityInternalStateServiceImpl / DataEntityServiceImpl / DataEntityGroupServiceImpl — none of the three RBAC primary-surface services). Single class-level mitigation (audit pattern added to all 3 services) closes the gap structurally.

Spring Boot 3.4.10's `management.endpoint.env.show-values: NEVER` DOES sanitise `/actuator/env` (so batch-C's actuator-env framing was overbroad); the durable leak surface is Lombok-generated `toString()` if logged. Per LSN-001 + LSN-002 case-law, this is the canonical insecure-default failure mode the ontology was built to surface.

  - **Notifications subsystem under-documented for operations (2026-05-12C + D)** — the live page documents channels + WAL requirements + cleanup but omits: no rate-limit (alert bursts → Slack 429 / SMTP queue saturation), no audit trail (operators can't answer "did this alert get delivered?"), no per-channel filtering (every channel gets every alert regardless of owner), no PII redaction (free-text descriptions flow verbatim into outbound payloads), replication-slot orphan risk on rename, dead `notifications.webhookUrl` top-level field still binds. **Batch D adds**: email port=0 (Java primitive default) cliff, boxed Boolean nullability, modern SMTP-AUTH OAUTH2 absent, no Reply-To/Cc/Bcc/DKIM support, sender no `@Email` validation, recipient list comma-split has no per-address trim (silent partial delivery from whitespace).
  - **NEW 2026-05-12D: Housekeeping subsystem doc completeness** — the live page describes "three cleanup tasks" but code has 5 HousekeepingJob beans (missing Activity + Message empty-partitions jobs); the `housekeeping.ttl.*` 30-day default lives only in the bundled `application.yml` (not in Java field initializers) — an operator overriding the YAML rebinds to 0 and triggers immediate hard-delete (LSN-001 shape). Docs acknowledge the jOOQ-precedence bug for manual RESOLVED alerts but provide no upstream-issue link / workaround.
  - **NEW 2026-05-12D: OAuth2 docs internal inconsistency** — the page uses `username-attribute` (no hyphen between user and name) in descriptive prose but `user-name-attribute` (hyphenated) in every YAML example; Spring relaxed binding maps `user-name-attribute` (not `username-attribute`) to the `userNameAttribute` POJO field. Operators copy-pasting the prose key get silent binding failure. Additionally, ODD_IAM provider is COMPLETELY absent from the docs page (5-enum-value vs 7-docs-providers drift gets a third angle) and `adminUserInfoFlag` (the ODD_IAM admin-detection mechanism) is undocumented despite being a POJO field.

## Findings

### HIGH severity

- **DOC-GAP-001**: DataEntity `/term` vs `/terms` path mismatch silently disables DATA_ENTITY_ADD_TERM and DATA_ENTITY_DELETE_TERM gates — undocumented on Permissions page
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__DataEntityController.md:concepts.invariants` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0]` (term path mismatch)
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Data Entity].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[0]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions` 2026-05-08 status 200 — page lists `DATA_ENTITY_ADD_TERM. Allows adding a term to a data entity` and `DATA_ENTITY_DELETE_TERM. Allows removing a term from a data entity` with NO warning that these gates are silently disabled by `/term` (SecurityConstants.java:237-242) vs `/terms` (DataEntityApi.java:148, 542) path mismatch.
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Data Entity].security_aggregate.authorization_consistency.detail`: "DATA_ENTITY_ADD_TERM and DATA_ENTITY_DELETE_TERM rules silently disabled by `/term` vs `/terms` path mismatch"
    - DataEntityController.md verifies any authenticated user can attach/detach terms on any entity.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add a "Known limitations" admonition to `configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions.md` next to DATA_ENTITY_ADD_TERM and DATA_ENTITY_DELETE_TERM rows: "Currently NOT enforced — the SECURITY_RULES path matcher uses `/term` while the API publishes `/terms`. Any authenticated user can attach/detach terms on any data entity until this is fixed in source." Cross-reference an upstream issue to fix the path matcher.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - LSN-class — silent-bypass of a documented permission gate (data-integrity at risk: arbitrary terms attached to any entity by any auth'd user)
    - Should drive a `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream fix in addition to the doc warning
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — operator reads permissions doc, believes terms are gated, deploys with assumption; in practice every authenticated user can mutate terms on every entity. LSN-001/LSN-002 class.

- **DOC-GAP-002**: Alerting feature page does not warn that `getAllAlerts` exposes every platform alert to any authenticated user; doc text names "stewards and admins" audience while code enforces any authenticated user
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__AlertController.md:security.known_security_gaps.[1]` (severity HIGH)
    - `odd-platform__java__AlertController__controller-method__changeAlertStatus.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0]`
    - `odd-platform__java__AlertController__controller-method__getAllAlerts.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0]` + `:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]` **(2026-05-10A — method-level audience-drift evidence)**
    - `odd-platform__openapi__tags__openapi-tag__alert.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0]`
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Alert].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[0,1]` + `:cross_file_inconsistencies.[2]` (doc-code audience divergence)
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/active-platform-features/alerting` 2026-05-10 status 200 — confirmed verbatim: "All — Every open and resolved alert across the whole platform" with use-case recommendation "Platform-wide triage; **stewards and admins watching the full alert surface**." The page contains **no discussion** of access-control, authorization, or role-based restrictions for the `getAllAlerts` endpoint or the "All" tab.
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/developer-guides/api-reference/alerts` 2026-05-08 status 200 — page lists `GET /api/alerts` and notes `My Objects`/`Dependents` require Owner-link, but does NOT warn that the `All` listing exposes every alert to every authenticated user.
    - getAllAlerts.md (2026-05-10A) verifies absence: `SecurityConstants.java:98-295` contains a rule for `PUT /api/alerts/{alert_id}/status` but NO entry for `GET /api/alerts`; `AuthorizationCustomizer.java:29-30` falls through to `pathMatchers('/**').authenticated()`. Repository `ReactiveAlertRepositoryImpl.java:143-145` issues `selectFrom(ALERT).where(ALERT.STATUS.eq(OPEN))` with no owner predicate.
    - **Audience-drift sub-finding (2026-05-10A)**: doc text recommends the All tab for "stewards and admins"; code permits any authenticated user. Two corrective paths exist (align doc to enforced "any authenticated user" behaviour, OR add an admin/steward Permission gate aligned with doc's stated audience) — the choice is the maintainer's; the drift is the finding.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add a "Visibility scope" admonition to `features/active-platform-features/alerting.md` and to `developer-guides/api-reference/alerts.md`: "`getAllAlerts` (`GET /api/alerts`) returns the entire platform's alert population to any authenticated user — there is no role/permission gate. The 'stewards and admins' framing on the All tab describes the *intended* audience, not an enforced restriction. Under `auth.type=DISABLED` the endpoint is anonymously reachable. If your deployment requires admin-only visibility on the `All` tab, gate the endpoint at the network layer or front the platform with a permission-aware reverse proxy." Mirror on `changeAlertStatus`: any authenticated user can mutate any alert by id. **Decide concurrently** whether to add a `ALERT_LIST_ALL` permission gate (code-side fix) or rewrite the doc audience framing (doc-side fix); either resolves the drift.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - LSN-001/LSN-002 class — operator follows doc trusting that "Alerts feature" is owner-scoped, deploys with no auth-mode-DISABLED expectation
    - F-053 (advisory locks) — same family of "operationally relevant default unsurfaced"
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — alert content carries entity identifiers, severity, slack-style messages; multi-tenant deployments leak cross-tenant alert metadata under default config. The audience-drift makes this worse: operators reading "stewards and admins" reasonably assume the platform enforces that scoping.

- **DOC-GAP-003**: AlertManager Webhook Receiver lacks rate-limit / payload-cap / dedup / spoofing caveats on operator-facing config page
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__AlertManagerController.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0,1,2,3]` (all severity HIGH)
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__AlertManagerController.md:performance.known_performance_gaps.[0,1,2]`
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[AlertManager Webhook Receiver].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[0,1,2,3]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-08 status 200 — Prometheus AlertManager Integration section is present and warns about no application-layer auth + recommends perimeter controls. Does NOT warn about: (a) entity_oddrn alert-spoofing (caller chooses arbitrary entity_oddrn, no check that caller may raise alerts on that entity); (b) no rate-limit / payload-size cap / no dedup — unauthenticated DoS vector creates unbounded `AlertPojo` + `AlertChunkPojo` rows; (c) silent orphaning when `entity_oddrn` label missing — payload accepted, persisted with null data_entity_oddrn, returns 204 No Content with no signal of misconfiguration; (d) ExternalAlert.startsAt timezone-naive `LocalDateTime` strips RFC3339 offsets.
    - AlertManagerController.md:security.known_security_gaps.[1] (Alert spoofing HIGH severity)
    - AlertManagerController.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0] (silent_orphan)
  - **Proposed doc action**: Extend the "Prometheus AlertManager Integration" section on `configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform.md` with a "Known limitations" admonition covering: spoofing surface (any caller within network reach can raise alerts on any entity by choosing entity_oddrn), no rate-limit / payload-size cap, no dedup of repeated AlertManager `group_interval` re-sends, silent orphaning when entity_oddrn missing (no visible caller signal), timezone-naive LocalDateTime drops AlertManager's RFC3339 offset, hand-rolled DTO does not honour AlertManager `status: resolved` to close alerts.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - LSN-002 family (operationally surprising default — webhook accepted-but-ignored)
    - Concept "AlertManager Webhook Receiver" is `canonical_candidate: true` per concepts.yaml — see DOC-GAP-019
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — webhook is the integration point operators wire production AlertManager into; missing caveats translate to (a) operator deploys without a rate-limit, gets DoS'd; (b) operator wires AlertManager without entity_oddrn validation, alerts silently orphan; (c) attacker on the network plane spoofs alerts onto sensitive entities.

- **DOC-GAP-004**: Attachment feature page does not warn about read-path authorization asymmetry (GET endpoints unprotected)
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__DataEntityAttachmentController.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0]` (severity HIGH; read-path asymmetry)
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__DataEntityAttachmentController.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[3]`

  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/attachments` 2026-05-08 status 200 — RBAC section names `DATA_ENTITY_ATTACHMENT_MANAGE` as the gate, but does NOT disclose that the gate is asymmetric: read paths (`GET /attachments`, `GET /files/uploads` upload options, `GET /files/{file_id}` download) carry no permission gate at all and are reachable by any authenticated user.
    - DataEntityAttachmentController sidecar verifies: "POST/PUT/DELETE on `/files/**` and `/links/**` gated by DATA_ENTITY_ATTACHMENT_MANAGE; GET endpoints carry NO permission gate."
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add to `features/data-discovery/attachments.md` RBAC section: "**Read-path posture**: The `DATA_ENTITY_ATTACHMENT_MANAGE` permission gates only the *write* paths (add, edit, delete, upload). The *read* paths (list attachments, download a file, fetch upload options) are gated by authentication only — any authenticated user can list and download attachments on any data entity. Under `auth.type=DISABLED` they are anonymously reachable."
  - **Cross-references**:
    - LSN-001 retrospective (already cited on the same page for LOCAL-ephemerality)
    - DataEntityController concept's same family (read-endpoints uniformly under-gated)
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — attachments may contain sensitive customer data; operator reading the doc believes attachments are gated.

- **DOC-GAP-005**: Attachment max-file-size cap is client-side-only; non-browser caller can submit arbitrary-size files — undocumented
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__AttachmentServiceImpl__config-key-consumer__attachment_max-file-size@L27.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]`
    - `odd-platform__java__AttachmentServiceImpl__config-key-consumer__attachment_max-file-size@L27.md:security.known_security_gaps`
    - `odd-platform__java__DataEntityAttachmentController__controller-method__uploadFileChunk.md:security.known_security_gaps.[3]` **(2026-05-10A — method-level reinforcement at HIGH severity)** + `:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1]`
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Attachment].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[1]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-08 status 200 — Attachment Storage Configuration section frames `attachment.max-file-size` as a per-file cap with `spring.codec.max-in-memory-size` interaction described as the WebFlux codec layer failure mode. Does NOT disclose the cap is enforced ONLY client-side (UI filter) — no service-layer re-validation in `AttachmentServiceImpl`, `DataEntityAttachmentController`, or `FileServiceImpl`.
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/attachments` 2026-05-10 status 200 — confirms the page asserts "Files larger than the cap are rejected by the upload API" — operator reasonably believes server enforces.
    - AttachmentServiceImpl@L27.md sidecar verifies absence of server-side enforcement.
    - **Method-level reinforcement (2026-05-10A)**: uploadFileChunk.md verifies the chunk endpoint reads no size, FileServiceImpl.java:58-67 calls `transferTo` without checking byte count. Combined with no rate-limit, this is a host-disk DOS surface against the chunk-staging filesystem. Already tracked as REFACTOR-013 (HIGH).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add to both pages (config + feature) a Known-limitations admonition: "**Server-side enforcement**: the `attachment.max-file-size` cap is enforced in the UI (the file-picker filters before upload). The chunked-upload API does not re-validate per-chunk or aggregate size, so a non-browser caller with `DATA_ENTITY_ATTACHMENT_MANAGE` can submit arbitrarily-large files. Operators who need a hard server-side cap must enforce it via `spring.codec.max-in-memory-size` (which fails the request at the WebFlux codec layer with `DataBufferLimitException`) or at the network layer."
  - **Cross-references**:
    - F-056 (`spring.codec.max-in-memory-size` undocumented) — fix in same content area
    - REFACTOR-013 in `lineage/odd-platform/refactoring-scopes.md:107-115`
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — operator-facing storage planning (S3 bucket size, LOCAL filesystem capacity) anchored on a cap that doesn't apply.

- **DOC-GAP-006**: `/actuator/env` exposes S3/MinIO credentials by default — undocumented on Attachment Storage page (**REFINED batch D**: Spring Boot 3.4.10's `show-values: NEVER` default DOES mask values; the durable leak surface is Lombok-toString — see DOC-GAP-067)
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__yaml__application_yml__config-prefix__attachment.md:security.known_security_gaps`
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Attachment].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[2]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-08 status 200 — Attachment Storage Configuration is comprehensive on storage backends and known limitations BUT contains NO warning about Spring Boot Actuator `/actuator/env` exposure surfacing `attachment.remote.access-key` / `attachment.remote.secret-key` keys.
    - attachment-config-prefix.md sidecar surfaces this as severity HIGH.
    - **Batch D refinement (2026-05-12)**: Per `ODDLDAPProperties.md` primary-source verification — Spring Boot 3.4.10's `management.endpoint.env.show-values: NEVER` default DOES sanitise `/actuator/env` (the secret values appear as `******`). The durable leak vector is NOT actuator-env (the actuator surface is partially protected by default); it is Lombok `@Data`-generated `toString()` if any code path logs the Properties bean. Need to verify whether the attachment-storage POJO (if any) is similarly `@Data`-decorated — likely yes by pattern. The actuator-env framing remains valid as a defence-in-depth concern (operators with externally-reachable actuator endpoints leak the key NAMES even when values are masked, and `show-values: NEVER` is operator-overridable).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Cross-link with F-054 (DOC-163) — when authoring the F-054 fix on Spring Boot Actuator, include a paragraph on attachment-storage credential exposure: "The default `management.endpoints.web.exposure.include` exposes `/actuator/env`. Spring Boot 3.4.10 masks credential VALUES by default (`management.endpoint.env.show-values: NEVER`), so resolved values appear as `******` — but the *key names* themselves (`attachment.remote.access-key`, `attachment.remote.secret-key`, `auth.oauth2.client.client-secret`, etc.) are still returned, confirming the configuration shape. **Additionally**, if any Spring `@ConfigurationProperties` POJO bound to these keys uses Lombok `@Data` (the platform's default pattern), the auto-generated `toString()` includes the credential field — any code path that logs the bean (or any future `log.debug("config = {}", properties)` addition) emits the credential verbatim, bypassing actuator masking entirely. For production deployments: (1) override the exposure list to drop `env`, or move Actuator to a separate management port; (2) ensure Properties classes carrying credentials use `@ToString.Exclude` on sensitive fields (see DOC-GAP-067)."
  - **Cross-references**:
    - **F-054** in `findings/docs-coverage-undocumented-features/2026-05-08.md` — same gap, broader scope (fold this finding into F-054's authoring as a sub-bullet)
    - **DOC-GAP-050** (LDAP `auth.ldap.password` leak — same actuator-vs-Lombok refinement; 2026-05-12C + D)
    - **DOC-GAP-067 (NEW batch D — META)** — Lombok-toString sensitive-field leak class; 4-sidecar triangulated
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — operationally-reachable Actuator + S3 credentials shape is a security-deployment footgun even with the value mask (key-name disclosure + `show-values` operator-overridable + Lombok-toString bypass).

- **DOC-GAP-007**: GenAI feature page lacks prompt-injection / SSRF / DISABLED-anonymous-reachability caveats
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__GenAIController.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0,1,2]` (all severity HIGH)
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_controller__controller__GenAIController.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1]`
