    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/policies` 2026-05-12 status 200 — same absence; the page focuses on policy JSON shape and does not mention the read-side discovery surface.
    - getResourcePermissions.md verifies: (a) `PermissionController.java:14-26` no `@PreAuthorize`, no `SECURITY_RULES` entry — read-side endpoint deliberately left out of `SECURITY_RULES` per `implicit_adrs.[3]` (read-collaborative posture: any authenticated user may query their own permissions; the response reveals only what the policy graph would grant the same caller anyway); (b) `PermissionServiceImpl.java:22-30` delegates to `ContextualPermissionExtractor`s; (c) the same `permissionService` bean is wired into `OAuthSecurityConfiguration.AuthorizationCustomizer` for mutation enforcement — read and enforce paths share one evaluation graph; (d) under DISABLED, `ReactiveSecurityContextHolder.getContext()` is empty, the chain cascades through `Mono.just(List.of())` to no permissions — UI shows "you can do nothing" while mutation endpoints are simultaneously `.permitAll()` — silent UI-vs-API contradiction; (e) under LOGIN_FORM, every caller authenticates as admin and EVERY `getResourcePermissions` call returns the full permission set regardless of policy revocations because the in-memory admin authority is static.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add a "Read-side discovery" H2 to `configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization.md` (the parent page) covering: (1) the `GET /api/resource/{permission_resource_type}/{resource_id}/permissions` endpoint contract; (2) the `PermissionResourceType` enum's four contextual values (DATA_ENTITY / TERM / QUERY_EXAMPLE / MANAGEMENT) — and explicitly note that **Lookup table is NOT a contextual resource type** (cross-link DOC-GAP-077); (3) the read-collaborative posture — the endpoint is gated by authentication only; reveals only the caller's own resolved permissions; this is the platform's deliberate stance per the codebase (point to the `SecurityConstants` rule absence on GET paths); (4) the UI's consumption pattern — every data-entity / term / query-example detail page renders buttons whose enabled state depends on this endpoint's response; (5) the LOGIN_FORM admin-static caveat — every form-authenticated user receives full permissions through this endpoint; (6) the DISABLED-mode contradiction — the endpoint returns empty while mutation endpoints are `.permitAll()`. Mirror a cross-link from `/authorization/permissions.md` and `/authorization/policies.md`.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-072, DOC-GAP-073, DOC-GAP-075 (sibling RBAC primary-surface docs gaps)

    - LSN-class — "intentional design but undocumented" silent posture
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — this is the UI's primary mechanism for deciding button-enabled state. Operators auditing `SecurityConstants.SECURITY_RULES` for read-side gating find no enforcement and may wrongly conclude the endpoint is unprotected; the docs do not record the design intent. Combined with the DISABLED contradiction (UI shows "you can do nothing"; API says "anyone can do anything"), the silence is operationally consequential.

- **DOC-GAP-079**: Search feature page (canonical `/features/data-discovery/search`) is silent on WHO can search + cross-owner catalog enumeration — the platform's WIDEST cross-owner read surface is undocumented (3rd corroborating surface for read-collaborative posture)

    - search.md verifies: (a) `POST /api/search` carries no `@PreAuthorize`, no `SECURITY_RULES` entry — falls through to `pathMatchers('/**').authenticated()`; (b) `SearchServiceImpl.java:74-82` runs `searchFacetRepository.create(pojo)` without owner predicate; `SearchServiceImpl.java:122-130` `countByState(state)` runs over the entire catalog; (c) `SearchServiceImpl.java:104-108` owner-filters ONLY when `state.isMyObjects()` is explicitly toggled; default is owner-bypass; (d) `GET /api/search/{search_id}/results` paginates every non-`EXCLUDE_FROM_SEARCH` data entity in the platform — name, ODDRN, descriptions, ownership, tags, custom metadata, term linkages; (e) under DISABLED, the endpoint is anonymously reachable (corollary of DOC-GAP-082 META — RBAC bypass under DISABLED extends to read surfaces with no SecurityRule).
    - Cross-cutting verification: this finding is the THIRD corroborating surface for the read-collaborative cross-owner enumeration invariant, joining DOC-GAP-002 (`getAllAlerts`) and DOC-GAP-025 (`getActivity`). Search is the WIDEST blast radius — catalog enumeration vector.

(2) **Owner-scoping toggle** — explicit `my_objects=true` filters to entities owned by the caller's associated Owner; this is opt-in, not the default. (3) **DISABLED-mode reachability** — under `auth.type=DISABLED` the endpoint is anonymously reachable; cross-link DOC-GAP-082 META and DOC-GAP-036 (DISABLED is the bundled default). (4) **Multi-tenant deployments** — operators expecting per-tenant search isolation must enforce it at the network or reverse-proxy layer. Pair with siblings DOC-GAP-002 (alerts) and DOC-GAP-025 (activity-feed) — the three pages need a consistent "Who can see this" treatment. Per the strengthened `Read-collaborative cross-owner enumeration` cross-cutting invariant in concepts.yaml, recommend authoring all three sections together as a single batch.

Same LSN-001-class shape as DOC-GAP-002 / DOC-GAP-025 (operator follows doc trusting that catalog is owner-scoped, deploys with broader visibility than expected).

    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim: "No discussion of how DISABLED mode interacts with Policy/Role/Owner enforcement" + "The page does not specify which authentication modes activate the authorization framework."
    - All 4 sub-pages (`/authorization/policies`, `/authorization/roles`, `/authorization/owners`, `/authorization/permissions`) WebFetched 2026-05-12 status 200 — all silent on DISABLED-mode bypass; 7 + 7 + 6 + 6 distinct topics not covered per individual DOC-GAPs.
    - Pattern shape verified across the 5 RBAC sidecars: `DisabledAuthSecurityConfiguration.java:13-18` calls `.anyExchange().permitAll()` and never wires `AuthorizationCustomizer`; consequence is that **every SECURITY_RULES entry is INERT under DISABLED**. The blast-radius enumeration from DOC-GAP-045 (CSRF / CORS / actuator / S2S-ignored / audit-absence / no-boot-WARN) now extends to RBAC primary mutations. Network-reachable DISABLED deployment + an attacker with a network plane = a MANAGEMENT/ALL policy, a custom Administrator-equivalent role, an attacker-controlled owner attached to it — all silently.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Single-page authoring action with cross-references. Extend the "Blast radius" section on `configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authentication/disabled-authentication.md` (as proposed in DOC-GAP-045) with an explicit "RBAC primary surface" sub-section: "**Under `auth.type=DISABLED`, the entire authorization framework is INERT.** `DisabledAuthSecurityConfiguration` calls `.anyExchange().permitAll()` and never wires `AuthorizationCustomizer` — every `SECURITY_RULES` entry, including the MANAGEMENT-tier `POLICY_CREATE` / `POLICY_UPDATE` / `POLICY_DELETE`, `ROLE_CREATE` / `ROLE_UPDATE` / `ROLE_DELETE`, `OWNER_CREATE` / `OWNER_UPDATE` / `OWNER_DELETE`, is bypassed. Any caller reachable on the network can: (1) `POST /api/policies` to author a MANAGEMENT/ALL policy identical in shape to the seeded Administrator; (2) `POST /api/roles` to bundle that policy under a name of their choosing; (3) `POST /api/owners` to create an Owner attached to that role; (4) optionally hard-delete the seeded `Administrator` row via direct SQL (out-of-band of the soft-delete API) and recreate it via `POST /api/policies` / `POST /api/roles` with attacker-chosen bindings — the Administrator-name reservation asymmetry on create (DOC-GAP-083 sibling pattern) makes this a complete escalation. **No audit trail is emitted** — `PolicyServiceImpl.create`, `RoleServiceImpl.create`, `OwnerServiceImpl.create` all silent (cross-link DOC-GAP-083 META). Operators who run an unmodified container image inherit DISABLED with no startup warning (cross-link DOC-GAP-036) and no log line (cross-link DOC-GAP-045 — no `@Slf4j`)." Pair with one-paragraph cross-references on each of the 5 affected `/authorization/*` live pages.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-036 (DISABLED-default of `auth.type` undocumented) — root cause
    - DOC-GAP-045 (DISABLED blast radius — this finding adds the RBAC primary-surface dimension to that blast radius)

    - DOC-GAP-079 (Search anonymously reachable under DISABLED — same root cause, different surface)
    - DOC-GAP-053 (META — "docs frame default behaviour but omit blast radius" — this is the catalog's largest instance)
    - DOC-GAP-083 (META — No-audit-log on RBAC mutations — companion gap; compounds DISABLED bypass because audit trail is absent under any mode)
    - LSN-001 / LSN-002 — canonical case-law class
    - REFACTOR-073 (default-DISABLED + no-fail-fast) in refactoring-scopes.md
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH (meta) — 8-sidecar triangulation. This is the largest "framing without blast radius" gap in the entire catalog: a single section addition on `disabled-authentication.md` plus 4 cross-references close the gap structurally across the entire RBAC primary surface (5 affected live pages). The keys-to-the-kingdom escalation chain is silent under DISABLED and silent under any other mode (no audit). Combined with DOC-GAP-036 (DISABLED-default of `auth.type` undocumented at the parent navigation), the net is: operators inherit DISABLED, never see a startup warning, never see a doc warning about RBAC bypass, never see an audit trail of the escalation. Highest-impact maintainer leverage in this catalog.

- **DOC-GAP-083**: **META-FINDING** — No-audit-log on RBAC mutations pattern (3-sidecar triangulated: Role + Policy + Owner; doc-side action requires 3 page admonitions; code-side fix is a uniform `@ActivityLog`/audit-table addition across 3 services)
  - **Category**: drift (meta)
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `createRole.md:security.known_security_gaps.[1]` (HIGH — no audit logging on role mutations) **(NEW batch E)**
    - `createPolicy.md:security.known_security_gaps.[1]` (HIGH — no audit logging on policy mutations) + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[1]` **(NEW batch E)**
    - `createOwner.md:security.known_security_gaps.[0]` (MEDIUM — no `@ActivityLog` on OwnerServiceImpl.create) + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[4]` **(NEW batch E)**
    - `concepts.yaml:invariants[No-audit-log on RBAC mutations]` (3-sidecar triangulated, version 6)
  - **Evidence**:
    - Pattern: every RBAC primary-surface service (`PolicyServiceImpl`, `RoleServiceImpl`, `OwnerServiceImpl`) is missing audit signals. Specific instances verified:
      - `PolicyServiceImpl.create` (lines 62-69), `.update` (lines 71-81), `.delete` (lines 83-95) — no `@Slf4j` log calls; no audit-table insert; no event publication. V0_0_55__add_policies_and_roles.sql contains no `policy_audit` / `policy_history` companion table.
      - `RoleServiceImpl.create` (lines 51-61), `.update` (lines 63-77), `.delete` (lines 79-92) — same absence pattern. No audit table.
      - `OwnerServiceImpl.create` (lines 55-66) — no `@ActivityLog` annotation (grep verified: `@ActivityLog` appears in `AlertHaltConfigServiceImpl`, `AlertServiceImpl`, `DataEntityInternalStateServiceImpl`, `DataEntityServiceImpl`, `DataEntityGroupServiceImpl` ONLY — none of the three RBAC primary-surface services).
    - Cross-axis verification: the platform's activity feed (`/api/activity` — Activity Feed concept from batch A) covers DataEntity / Alert / DataEntityInternalState activity but does NOT extend to Role / Policy / Permission / Owner-directory mutations.
    - WebFetch of each `/authorization/{roles,policies,owners,permissions}` page 2026-05-12 confirms NONE of the four pages warns operators about the RBAC audit-trail gap; `/authorization` parent page is also silent.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Three-part action.
    1. **Doc-side**: add a "Audit trail" admonition to each of the three RBAC primary-surface pages (`enable-security/authorization/policies.md`, `roles.md`, `owners.md`): "**RBAC mutations are not audit-logged**. `POST` / `PUT` / `DELETE` operations on `/api/{policies,roles,owners}` emit no application-log line, publish no event, and INSERT into no audit-history table. The platform's activity feed (`GET /api/activity`) does NOT cover RBAC mutations — only DataEntity-side state changes. A security-incident reviewer asking 'who authored / modified this MANAGEMENT/ALL policy on date X?' cannot answer from the running platform; the operator must consult external DB-side audit (Postgres `pg_audit` or a similar mechanism). For audit-required deployments, configure DB-side query logging on the `policy`, `role`, `role_to_policy`, `owner`, and `owner_to_role` tables before promoting to production."
    2. **Code-side upstream**: file `/log-issue odd-platform` with three options ordered by scope: (a) **Minimum**: add `@Slf4j` log.info to PolicyServiceImpl.create / RoleServiceImpl.create / OwnerServiceImpl.create — minimum-cost log-only audit. (b) **Medium**: add `@ActivityLog` annotations matching the existing pattern (AlertServiceImpl + DataEntityServiceImpl); requires extending `ActivityEventType` enum to cover RBAC mutations + extending `ActivityState` renderer for Role/Policy/Owner payloads. (c) **Full**: introduce a dedicated `rbac_audit` append-only table written via `BaseSoftDeleteCRUDRepository`'s mutation lifecycle hooks; full forensic trail; coordinates with the existing soft-delete pattern.
    3. **Pillar-side meta-recommendation**: add to `pillars/documentation/gates.md` a reviewer checklist item: "For any doc page describing a mutating RBAC operation (Policy / Role / Owner / Permission), verify the page includes an Audit-trail caveat naming the absence of in-application audit logging (or — once fixed — naming the audit mechanism)."
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-072, DOC-GAP-073, DOC-GAP-075 — the 3 affected RBAC primary-surface doc pages (audit-absence is a sub-finding of each)
    - DOC-GAP-082 (META — DISABLED-bypasses-RBAC; the audit gap compounds the DISABLED gap: under DISABLED, escalation is silent and trail-free)
    - DOC-GAP-053 (META — docs frame defaults without blast radius; adjacent class)
    - LSN-001 / LSN-002 — operator-impact-by-omission class
    - Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream for audit-log mechanism across 3 services
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH (meta) — 3-sidecar triangulated; the highest-leverage gap in the RBAC surface because every other security weakness assumes attack visibility via logs (this one says logs don't exist). Compounds DOC-GAP-082 META: under DISABLED, escalation is silent (no auth check) AND trail-free (no audit). Operators committing to ODD as their authorization fabric need to understand that the platform's audit story for RBAC mutations is "configure DB-side audit yourself"; the docs currently leave this entirely implicit. Single class-level mitigation (audit pattern added to all 3 services + 3 doc admonitions) closes the gap structurally.

- **DOC-GAP-097**: `PUT /api/dataentities/{id}/description` is a pure UPDATE with silent no-op on missing entity — operationId, OpenAPI summary, and consumer expectation all use "upsert" language that contradicts the implementation
  - **Category**: drift (OpenAPI contract drift; spec asserts upsert; implementation is replace-or-silently-200)
  - **Surfaced by**: `upsertDataEntityInternalDescription.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases[1]` + `upsertDataEntityInternalDescription.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[2]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `openapi.yaml:929-930` — operation summary: "Upsert DataEntity's internal description in markdown format"; operationId: `upsertDataEntityInternalDescription`.
    - `ReactiveDataEntityRepositoryImpl.java:432-435` — query is `DSL.update(...).set(...).where(...).returning()` — pure UPDATE; no INSERT branch; no existence check.
    - On missing `dataEntityId`: `Mono.empty`, pipeline short-circuits, controller returns 200 OK with empty body — NOT 404.
    - Contrast: sibling `DataEntityServiceImpl.updateStatus` (line 467) DOES call `.switchIfEmpty(() -> Mono.error(new NotFoundException("DataEntity", id)))`.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Two-front fix: (a) on OpenAPI side, rename op to `replaceDataEntityInternalDescription` + add 404 response OR add `.switchIfEmpty(NotFoundException)`; (b) on docs side, add caveat to description doc page about endpoint requiring entity exists.
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — silent-success-on-wrong-id is a Class-A operator trap. LSN-001-shape.

- **DOC-GAP-098**: `createDataEntityTagsRelations` operationId is misleading — semantic is replace-all (delete missing) but spec/operationId/method-name say "create" (additive); third-party consumers will silently lose tags
  - **Category**: drift (OpenAPI contract drift; create-language for replace-all behaviour)
  - **Surfaced by**: `createDataEntityTagsRelations.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases[1]` + `createDataEntityTagsRelations.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[1]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `openapi.yaml:1173-1175` — operation summary "Creates tags relations for DataEntity entity" + operationId `createDataEntityTagsRelations` + description "Creates tags relations tags for DataEntity entity. Also creates corresponding tags in the system if they don't exist."
    - `TagServiceImpl.java:113-120` — actual logic: `pojosToDelete = current - updated` + `deleteDataEntityRelations(toDelete).thenMany(createDataEntityRelations(updated))` — replace-all-internal-tags.
    - `odd-platform-ui/src/redux/thunks/dataentities.thunks.ts:46` — UI redux action correctly named `updateDataEntityTagsActionType` (UI authors understood; spec authors did not).
    - External tag relations (`tag_to_data_entity.external = true`) ARE preserved (`.filter(pojo -> !pojo.getExternal())` line 102) — undocumented entirely.
  - **Proposed doc action**: (a) Rename OpenAPI operation to `replaceDataEntityInternalTagsRelations`; (b) explicit description block covering: REPLACE-ALL semantics on INTERNAL tags; EXTERNAL preservation; auto-create-on-miss; `important = false` default; `TAG_CREATE` scope-asymmetry.
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — ingestion-pipeline mistake by third-party API consumer using only the spec would silently delete data entity's internal tag history. LSN-001 class.

- **DOC-GAP-099**: `getMyObjectsWithUpstream` / `getMyObjectsWithDownstream` OpenAPI summary literally describes the wrong semantic — claims response is owned-with-lineage; actual response is NON-owned entities reachable from owned set
  - **Category**: drift (OpenAPI contract drift; spec summary is the inverse of implementation)
  - **Surfaced by**: `getMyObjects.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[2]` + `getMyObjects.md:implicit_adrs[3]` + `getMyObjects.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases[5]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `openapi.yaml:842-844` — summary for `getMyObjectsWithUpstream`: "Returns list of data entities owned by current user with upstream dependencies"
    - `openapi.yaml:860-862` — parallel for `getMyObjectsWithDownstream`.
    - `DataEntityRelationsServiceImpl.java:25-31` — actual flow: (a) fetch user's owned entities (anchor), (b) traverse lineage one hop, (c) filter to entities NOT in owned set (`Predicate.not(oddrns::contains)` line 37) — response is entities the user does NOT own but ARE reachable from entities they DO own.
    - Lineage variants use different code path than `getMyObjects`: NO direct SQL owner-filter at response layer; owner-scoping implicit via anchor-set correctness.
  - **Proposed doc action**: (a) Rewrite OpenAPI summary on both methods to: "Returns data entities reachable in one lineage hop from entities owned by the current user (excludes owned entities themselves)"; (b) document the security-relevant consequence: response is data-ecosystem context, NOT owned set; (c) add developer-guide note that owner-scoping for these two methods is anchor-set correctness (not a downstream SQL filter).
  - **Severity rationale**: HIGH — security-impact gap. Operator believing they're returning owner-scoped results (per OpenAPI summary) might publish endpoints in multi-tenant deployment expecting tenant isolation; instead returning cross-tenant lineage neighbours. The summary is the INVERSE of behaviour.

- **DOC-GAP-057**: Notifications subsystem under-documents operational caveats — dead `notifications.webhookUrl` field, no per-channel filtering, no PII redaction, replication-slot orphan risk on rename, webhook unsigned delivery
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `NotificationsProperties.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0,7,9,12,13]` (multiple MEDIUM/HIGH) + `:security.known_security_gaps.[0,2]` **(new 2026-05-12C)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Notifications]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/active-platform-features/notifications` 2026-05-12 status 200 — page documents channels + lifecycle + SMTP/AlertManager caveats but is silent on the 5 sub-caveats below.
    - NotificationsProperties.md verifies five distinct sub-findings:
      - (1) **Dead `notifications.webhookUrl` field**: `NotificationsProperties.java:9` declares a top-level `webhookUrl` String field; no code in the notification package reads it (grep negative 2026-05-12). The active webhook URL is `notifications.receivers.webhook.url`. An operator setting the top-level key gets silent acceptance, zero effect.
      - (2) **No per-channel filtering**: `AlertNotificationMessageProcessor.java:25-36` iterates `List<NotificationSender>` unconditionally on every alert. No filter by alert type, severity, data-entity owner, or namespace. An operator wanting "only Critical alerts to Slack, all to email" cannot express this in config.
      - (3) **No PII redaction**: `AlertNotificationMessageTranslator.java:73-83` populates the full alert payload — dataEntity name, dataSourceName, namespaceName, owners[], downstream lineage entities, alertChunks — into Slack/webhook/email outbound. Free-text descriptions / business names / customer-id-encoded table names flow verbatim to outbound channels with no redaction option.
      - (4) **Replication-slot orphan risk on rename**: `NotificationSubscriber.java:99-122` lazy-creates the replication slot but never drops it; if an operator renames `notifications.wal.replication-slot-name` between deploys and forgets to drop the old slot, Postgres retains WAL for the orphan indefinitely — risking primary disk exhaustion. The live doc warns about manual cleanup but does NOT warn about rename-orphan specifically.
      - (5) **Webhook unsigned delivery**: `WebhookNotificationSender.java:18-23` is a plain `HttpRequest.POST(...)` with no HMAC, no shared secret, no signature header. Receivers cannot verify that a webhook actually originated from ODD Platform vs an attacker who scraped the webhook URL.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add to `features/active-platform-features/notifications.md` a "Known limitations and operational caveats" section enumerating all five caveats with the proximate consequence + mitigation for each. Pair with code-side cleanup of dead `webhookUrl` field via `/log-issue odd-platform`.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-054 (rate-limit) + DOC-GAP-055 (audit trail) — these three findings together (DOC-GAP-054 / DOC-GAP-055 / DOC-GAP-057) are the operational-caveat cluster on the Notifications page; recommend authoring them as one consolidated "Known limitations" section
    - Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream for: (a) drop dead `webhookUrl` field, (b) per-channel routing config, (c) webhook signing / HMAC option
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — none of the five sub-caveats is independently HIGH (PII redaction is operator-controllable; webhook signing is industry-standard but not always required; dead field is config-hygiene), but the cluster's collective impact on operational confidence is significant. The PII surface in particular matters for regulated deployments.

- **DOC-GAP-058**: **META-FINDING** — GitBook legacy-vs-canonical routing drift is a cross-cutting class (**now 3-sidecar triangulated after batch E: DataCollaboration + Notifications + Search**); recommend a doc-side audit of ALL legacy paths
  - **Category**: broken-url
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `postMessageInSlack.md:docs_link_semantic.inferred_docs.[0]` (DataCollaboration legacy 404 — batch 2026-05-10A)
    - `NotificationsProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.inferred_docs.[2]` + `:doc_drift_findings.[0]` (Notifications legacy 404 — batch 2026-05-12C)
    - `search.md:docs_link_semantic.inferred_docs.[1]` (status 404; `/features/active-platform-features/search` — batch 2026-05-12E) **(NEW batch E — strengthens to 3-sidecar)**
    - Pattern referenced in concepts.yaml's batch-C cross-cutting findings comment block
    - All individual instances: DOC-GAP-011..015 + DOC-GAP-035 + DOC-GAP-056 + DOC-GAP-081
  - **Evidence**:
    - Pattern: every URL of the form `/active-platform-features/{slug}` or `/data-discovery/{slug}` or `/features/active-platform-features/{slug-that-actually-lives-under-features/data-discovery}` or `/main-concepts` 404s with a GitBook redirect-suggestion stub. The canonical paths fall under `/features/active-platform-features/{slug}` OR `/features/data-discovery/{slug}` OR `/introduction/main-concepts.md`. **Batch E adds a new sub-shape**: the search slug exists under `/features/data-discovery/search` but the natural-guess `/features/active-platform-features/search` does NOT — a cross-pillar drift (some features live under `data-discovery`, others under `active-platform-features`, the IA boundary is not obvious from feature name alone).
    - 3-sidecar triangulation across batches A, C, and E confirms the pattern is generalisable, not single-page noise. Recommend treating as a class-level concern. The cross-pillar drift (search lives in data-discovery, not active-platform-features) is worth a separate IA-decision admonition on the docs side.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Three-part class-level fix (strengthened by batch E):
    1. **Doc-side audit**: Sweep the `documentation/` repo for any internal links pointing at the legacy paths (`/active-platform-features/*`, `/data-discovery/*`, `/main-concepts`). Update to canonical paths. Verify via `git grep` in the docs repo.
    2. **External-link mitigation**: For each legacy path that's likely to surface in external blog posts / Slack discussions / GitHub README hyperlinks, add a GitBook redirect rule in `.gitbook.yaml` (GitBook supports path redirects). **Recommended set updated batch E**: alerting, genai, data-collaboration, notifications, activity-feed (all under `active-platform-features/`); attachments, directory, **search** (under `data-discovery/`); plus `/main-concepts`. The `search` redirect should cover BOTH `/active-platform-features/search` and `/features/active-platform-features/search` → `/features/data-discovery/search`.
    3. **Substrate / scanner / state-file fix**: Any sidecar / scanner / state file pointing at legacy URLs needs an update on next enrichment.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - All individual broken-url findings: DOC-GAP-011..015 + DOC-GAP-035 + DOC-GAP-056 + DOC-GAP-081
    - Concept "Notifications" + "Slack collaboration app" + "Search Session" in concepts.yaml (all have cross_file_inconsistencies entries naming this drift)
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM (meta — the underlying broken-URL rubric is MEDIUM); the class is worth surfacing as a single audit-recommendation rather than 8 individual same-shape findings. Batch E's 3-sidecar strengthening materially supports the recommendation.

- **DOC-GAP-060**: Housekeeping docs frame the subsystem as "three cleanup tasks" but code has 5 HousekeepingJob beans — `ActivityEmptyPartitionsHousekeepingJob` and `MessageEmptyPartitionsHousekeepingJob` are undocumented
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_housekeeping_config__config-properties-class__HousekeepingTTLProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1]` **(NEW batch D)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Housekeeping TTL]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim: "...iterates through three cleanup tasks: resolved alerts, search-facet history, and soft-deleted data entities."
    - HousekeepingTTLProperties.md verifies code reality: `HousekeepingJobManager` iterates `List<HousekeepingJob>` — Spring autowires FIVE implementations: `AlertHousekeepingJob`, `SearchFacetsHousekeepingJob`, `DataEntityHousekeepingJob`, plus `ActivityEmptyPartitionsHousekeepingJob` and `MessageEmptyPartitionsHousekeepingJob` (both extend `EmptyPartitionsHousekeepingJob`). The two empty-partitions jobs run on the same 15-minute schedule and drop empty past partitions for the activity / message tables.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Update the housekeeping section's enumeration to "five cleanup tasks" — three row-by-age DELETE jobs (alerts / search-facets / data-entities) plus two empty-partitions DROP jobs (activity / message). Cross-link the empty-partitions jobs to the partition-period config keys (`odd.activity.partition-period`, `datacollaboration.message-partition-period`) and clarify that empty-partition-drop ≠ row-by-age retention (the partitions must be already empty — see DOC-GAP-041, DOC-GAP-061).
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-041 (activity retention claim drift); DOC-GAP-061 (message retention gap); LSN-001 class (partial-coverage docs).
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — operators reading the housekeeping section cannot learn that the same scheduler also drops empty activity/message partitions; the gap is incomplete-coverage rather than incorrect-content.

- **DOC-GAP-062**: AlertHousekeepingJob jOOQ-precedence bug acknowledged in docs but unlinked to a tracking issue / no workaround documented
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_housekeeping_config__config-properties-class__HousekeepingTTLProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[3]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[3]` (severity HIGH per sidecar — silent data loss for manual alert resolutions) **(NEW batch D)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Housekeeping TTL]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim: "**Note:** the retention window is intended to apply to both `RESOLVED` (manual) and `RESOLVED_AUTOMATICALLY` (system) states, but a **known platform bug currently exempts manual resolutions from the retention check** — manual `RESOLVED` alerts are hard-deleted on the next housekeeping run regardless of this value."
    - HousekeepingTTLProperties.md verifies: `AlertHousekeepingJob.java:28-34` — predicate is `.where(STATUS.eq(RESOLVED)).or(STATUS.eq(RESOLVED_AUTOMATICALLY)).and(STATUS_UPDATED_AT.lessOrEqual(cutoff))`. jOOQ's operator precedence: `.and(...)` binds to the most recent `.or(...)`, producing `(STATUS=RESOLVED) OR (STATUS=RESOLVED_AUTOMATICALLY AND STATUS_UPDATED_AT <= cutoff)`. Manual RESOLVED rows lack the TTL guard and are hard-deleted on the very next 15-minute cycle. The bug acknowledgement is useful but the page provides NO upstream-issue link, NO workaround, NO ETA for fix.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Update the bug-acknowledgement paragraph on the housekeeping section to link to an upstream issue: "...a known platform bug (see [odd-platform issue #NNNN](https://github.com/opendatadiscovery/odd-platform/issues/NNNN)) currently exempts manual resolutions from the retention check..." File `/log-issue odd-platform` first to create the tracking issue with: (a) the bug repro, (b) the suggested fix (`.where(STATUS.eq(RESOLVED).or(STATUS.eq(RESOLVED_AUTOMATICALLY))).and(STATUS_UPDATED_AT.lessOrEqual(cutoff))`), (c) the operator workaround (disable housekeeping until fixed: `housekeeping.enabled: false` — but this stops ALL three jobs, not just alerts).
  - **Cross-references**: LSN-001 class; Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` for the jOOQ-fix.
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — the docs are honest about the bug but leave operators with no tracking link or workaround. For a documented bug, the operator-impact is mitigated by awareness; the docs gap is in tracking/recovery, not in concealment.

- **DOC-GAP-064**: DataCollaboration lock-id collision risk undocumented — operators tuning the four advisory-lock IDs (`partition.advisory-lock-id=90`, `notifications.wal.advisory-lock-id=100`, `datacollaboration.receive-event-advisory-lock-id=110`, `datacollaboration.sender-message-advisory-lock-id=120`) get no guardrails; operator who copies default 100 to data-collab silently breaks both subsystems
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_datacollaboration_config__config-properties-class__DataCollaborationProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[2]` + `:security.known_security_gaps.[1]` (severity MEDIUM) **(NEW batch D)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Slack collaboration app]` + `:invariants[advisory-lock-collision]` (NEW batch D)
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/active-platform-features/data-collaboration` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim verdict: "This page does **not warn about setting lock IDs to colliding values** or mention conflict risks between subsystems."
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-12 status 200 — Enable Data Collaboration section enumerates the lock-IDs with their defaults (110, 120) but does NOT mention that operators MUST keep them disjoint from `partition.advisory-lock-id` (90) and `notifications.wal.advisory-lock-id` (100). No collision-avoidance guidance.
    - DataCollaborationProperties.md verifies: `DataCollaborationProperties.java:14-20` validates `sendingMessagesRetryCount >= 0` only; there is ZERO cross-subsystem validation that the four advisory-lock-ids are disjoint. Grep `<odd-platform>` for `advisoryLockId` collision checks returned no hits 2026-05-12. An operator who sets `datacollaboration.sender-message-advisory-lock-id: 100` silently overlaps `notifications.wal.advisory-lock-id` (default 100) — both subsystems contend on the same Postgres advisory lock; whichever boots first wins, the other silently fails to acquire its lock and produces no warning.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Two-part fix. (1) Doc-side: add a "Lock-ID disjointness" admonition to the Enable Data Collaboration section AND to the feature page: "**Advisory lock IDs must be disjoint across subsystems**. ODD Platform uses four Postgres advisory locks for leader-elected background workers — `partition.advisory-lock-id` (default 90, partition CREATE job), `notifications.wal.advisory-lock-id` (default 100, alert-notification WAL subscriber), `datacollaboration.receive-event-advisory-lock-id` (default 110, Slack event receiver), `datacollaboration.sender-message-advisory-lock-id` (default 120, Slack message sender). The shipped defaults are non-overlapping; if you override ANY of these, ensure your custom values remain disjoint across ALL FOUR subsystems. There is NO boot-time validation — colliding lock IDs silently disable one of the colliding subsystems with no log warning." (2) Code-side: file `/log-issue odd-platform` to add cross-subsystem validation at boot — a `@PostConstruct` check that asserts the four advisory-lock-ids are pairwise distinct.
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-053 (META: docs frame default behaviour but omit blast radius — adjacent shape); Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` for cross-subsystem lock-id-disjointness validation.
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — operationally significant; collision produces silent feature degradation. Severity bounded by the fact that operators customising lock-ids without a reason are rare; the failure mode is invisible-failure on a tuneable knob, not a default-shipped bug.

- **DOC-GAP-066**: Email channel config doc completeness — `port`=int default 0 cliff, boxed Boolean nullability, modern SMTP-AUTH OAUTH2 absent, no Reply-To / Cc / Bcc / DKIM support, sender no `@Email` validation, recipient list comma-split has no per-address trim
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_notification_config__config-properties-class__EmailSenderProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1,2]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[5,6,8,9,10,11]` (severities mostly LOW/MEDIUM individually; cluster MEDIUM) **(NEW batch D)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Notifications]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim verdict from this session's fetch: (a) `port` type/default-0 NOT documented; (b) `smtp.auth` / `smtp.starttls` boxed-Boolean nullability NOT documented; (c) modern OAUTH2/XOAUTH2 SMTP-AUTH NOT documented; (d) Reply-To / Cc / Bcc / DKIM NOT documented; (e) sender `@Email` validation NOT documented; (f) recipient list comma-split per-address trimming NOT documented; `mail.smtp.ssl.trust` workaround IS documented (positive coverage).
    - EmailSenderProperties.md verifies code-side specifics:
      - `EmailSenderProperties.java:12` — `private int port;` (primitive; defaults to 0)
      - `EmailSenderProperties.java:18-20` — `private Boolean auth;` / `private Boolean starttls;` (boxed; null at binding time is legal; `Properties#put(\"mail.smtp.auth\", null)` throws NPE at first SMTP send, leaking past boot validation)
      - `NotificationConfiguration.java:51-72` — no auth-mechanism keys, no `mail.smtp.connectionpoolsize`, no `mail.smtp.ssl.enable` (only STARTTLS)
      - `EmailNotificationSender.java:51,55` — only `setSubject` + `setTo` called; no Reply-To / Cc / Bcc / DKIM
      - `NotificationConfiguration.java:39-41` — `StringUtils.isBlank` check only on sender; no `@Email` validation
      - `NotificationConfiguration.java:118` — recipient list `notificationEmails.trim().split(\",\")` — no per-address trim; leading-space recipient becomes `' b@x.com'` (invalid InternetAddress)
  - **Proposed doc action**: Extend the Enable Alert Notifications section's email-config sub-list with a "Known limitations" admonition cluster covering all six caveats. Each caveat should name (a) the symptom an operator sees, (b) the proximate code site (just the file path, not line numbers, in doc text), (c) the mitigation. Example: "`port` is a Java `int` primitive — omitting the key binds to `0` and JavaMail falls back to the protocol default port. To set port=587 explicitly, ensure the YAML key is present. For SMTP-AUTH OAUTH2 (XOAUTH2) modern auth (Microsoft 365 / Gmail OAUTH2-only relays), this channel does not support it; use the webhook channel instead or implement an OAUTH2-to-PLAIN-SMTP proxy." Mirror on `features/active-platform-features/notifications.md`.
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-054 (rate-limit absence); DOC-GAP-055 (audit trail absence); DOC-GAP-057 (operational caveat cluster); DOC-GAP-067 (Lombok-toString — email password leak vector).
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — cluster of six operationally-relevant caveats, each individually LOW but collectively significant for an operator deploying email-channel notifications in production. Recipient-list comma-split (point f) is the highest-impact within the cluster because it produces silent partial delivery (the cluster joins DOC-GAP-054/055/057 as the Notifications-page completeness gap).

- **DOC-GAP-068**: **META-FINDING** — Partial-home pattern: `@ConfigurationProperties` POJOs bind only a subset of their config-prefix's keys; docs that enumerate the prefix don't surface the @Value-scattered remainder
  - **Category**: drift (meta)
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_datacollaboration_config__config-properties-class__DataCollaborationProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[3]` (severity LOW per sidecar) — 3 of 7 keys (NEW batch D)
    - `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_notification_config__config-properties-class__EmailSenderProperties.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[8]` (severity LOW per sidecar) — recipient list scattered (NEW batch D)
    - `concepts.yaml:invariants[Partial-home pattern]` (2-sidecar triangulated; version 5)
  - **Evidence**:
    - Pattern: `@ConfigurationProperties` POJOs that ought to be the single source-of-truth for a config-prefix bind only some of the prefix's keys; the rest are read via `@Value` injection scattered across other classes. Specific instances verified:
      - `DataCollaborationProperties` binds 3 of 7 `datacollaboration.*` keys (`sender-message-advisory-lock-id`, `receive-event-advisory-lock-id`, `sending-messages-retry-count`); remainder (`enabled`, `slack-oauth-token`, `message-partition-period`) read via `@Value` across `DataCollaborationConfiguration.java:21` + `FeatureResolverImpl.java:17` + `MessageTablePartitionManager.java:19`.
      - `EmailSenderProperties` binds 7 fields under `notifications.receivers.email.*` but does NOT model `notifications.receivers.email.notification.emails` (recipient list); the recipient list is consumed via `@Value` at `NotificationConfiguration.java:104`.
    - Doc impact: pages that enumerate the prefix as a coherent surface (the configuration guide does this for both data-collab and notifications) don't surface that ONE POJO doesn't cover ALL keys. A maintainer onboarding to extend the feature reads the Properties class to understand the schema and misses the scattered keys. An operator reading the docs sees the prefix as coherent; the operator-impact is LOW (docs are accurate, the prefix DOES work as documented). The maintainer-impact is MEDIUM (config-prefix has no single class to point at).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Two-part fix per affected feature. (1) Doc-side: when authoring the config-prefix enumeration in the configuration guide, prefix the list with "**Where these keys are bound**: most of this prefix is bound via `<canonical-POJO>`. A few keys are consumed via `@Value` directly: `<scattered-keys-list>` are read at boot in `<consumer-class>`. The docs treat the prefix as a single surface; in source code the prefix is split — bear this in mind when extending the feature." (2) Code-side upstream: file `/log-issue odd-platform` per affected feature to consolidate scattered `@Value` reads into the canonical Properties class. Recommended order: (a) DataCollab → add `enabled` + `slackOauthToken` + `messagePartitionPeriod` to `DataCollaborationProperties`; (b) Notifications → add `notification.emails` to `EmailSenderProperties` or to a sibling recipient-list POJO.
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-053 (META: docs frame defaults without blast radius — adjacent pattern); DOC-GAP-071 (DataCollab-specific instance); Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream for partial-home consolidation.
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM (meta) — 2-sidecar triangulation suggests the pattern is broader (likely affects other `@ConfigurationProperties` POJOs in the codebase). Operator-impact is LOW per instance; maintainer-onboarding-impact is MEDIUM cumulative.

  - **Surfaced by**:

- **DOC-GAP-074**: OpenAPI declares 201 Created for `POST /api/owners` (and sibling create endpoints) but `OwnerController.java:26` returns 200 OK via `ResponseEntity::ok` — third concrete instance of a class-wide 201-vs-200 OpenAPI/implementation drift on RBAC create operations
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__OwnerController__controller-method__createOwner.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[0]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[0]` (severity MEDIUM) **(NEW batch E)**
    - `odd-platform__java__RoleController__controller-method__createRole.md:tests_coverage_semantic.uncovered_behaviours.[0]` (sibling drift — RoleController also declares 201 in `openapi.yaml:3629` but returns 200) **(NEW batch E)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Owner (Authorization directory entry)].cross_file_inconsistencies.[0]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `openapi.yaml` `/api/owners` POST declares `responses.201` (Created); generated `OwnerApi.java:57` carries `@ApiResponse(responseCode = '201')`.
    - `OwnerController.java:26` — `.map(ResponseEntity::ok)` returns HTTP 200 OK.
    - createOwner.md notes: "Sibling create operations have the same pattern (e.g. `updateOwner` declares 201 in OpenAPI for an update — itself unusual — and the controller returns 200)." RoleController.createRole exhibits the same drift (openapi.yaml:3629 declares 201; `RoleController.java:24` returns 200).
    - Class-wide pattern verified across at least 3 RBAC create operations (Owner + Role + Policy — Policy's spec assertion not separately verified in this pass but the controller pattern at PolicyController.java:25 follows the same `.map(ResponseEntity::ok)` shape).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Single upstream fix — file `/log-issue odd-platform` (or extend an existing OpenAPI-vs-impl drift issue) to either (a) update controller returns to `ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.CREATED).body(...)` to match the spec, OR (b) update the OpenAPI spec `responses.201` → `responses.200` to match the implementation. Recommended (a) — `POST` returning 201 Created is the REST convention and the OpenAPI page is the authoritative consumer contract. The fix is class-wide: audit every `*Controller.create*` method against its `openapi.yaml` `responses.201` declaration. Doc-side: no immediate page-level action; the spec/codegen mismatch is operationally visible to OpenAPI consumers (the OpenAPI Swagger UI page declares 201 verbatim).
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-018 (API spec carries no security block — same class of spec-vs-runtime drift)
    - createRole.md:tests_coverage_semantic.uncovered_behaviours.[0] (sibling drift on Role)
    - Drives `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream for the spec-vs-impl alignment audit
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — operationally visible to OpenAPI clients (codegen produces a method signature expecting 201; runtime receives 200). Contract clients that test for `2xx` succeed; clients that strictly check `status == 201` fail. The class-wide pattern (3+ instances) makes this worth a single audit pass rather than per-endpoint fixes.

- **DOC-GAP-075**: Owners live doc page omits creation mechanics (`POST /api/owners`), `OWNER_CREATE` permission, audit-trail absence, association-request flow mechanics, name validation gaps, and soft-delete recovery semantics (6 doc-drift sub-findings)
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__OwnerController__controller-method__createOwner.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1,2]` (MEDIUM per sidecar; the page page-level audit explicitly enumerates 4 absences from the live page) + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[1-5]` + `:security.known_security_gaps.[0-3]` **(NEW batch E)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Owner (Authorization directory entry)]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/owners` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim re-verified this session: page documents what an Owner is ("Data Owners — people who manage and maintain a particular data entity or term") and the management UI surface ("Management → Owners tab"); confirms user-association expectation. **NONE of the following are addressed**: (1) creating an Owner via API/POST, (2) the `OWNER_CREATE` permission, (3) name uniqueness / format / validation, (4) audit logging or activity-feed events for Owner creation, (5) what happens on first login (auto-create vs manual association), (6) recovery from accidental Owner deletion (soft-delete behaviour of name uniqueness).
    - createOwner.md verifies: (a) `POST /api/owners` gated by `SecurityConstants.java:143` `OWNER_CREATE` NO_CONTEXT; (b) `OwnerServiceImpl.create` (lines 55-66) — no `@ActivityLog` (verified by grep — only AlertHaltConfig / Alert / DataEntityInternalState / DataEntity / DataEntityGroup carry it); (c) `owner.name varchar(255) NOT NULL UNIQUE` is plain (non-partial) unique — a soft-deleted owner permanently holds the name (`V0_0_89__update_user_owner.sql:9-15` applied partial-unique to user_owner_mapping but NOT to owner); (d) `@NotNull` on `name` but NO `@NotBlank` / `@Size` / `@Pattern` — empty / whitespace / homoglyph / control-character names accepted; (e) no auth flow auto-creates Owner on login — `getOrCreate` invoked ONLY from `OwnerAssociationRequestServiceImpl.createOwnerAssociationRequest` (manual user-initiated association); (f) cross-cutting Administrator-name reservation asymmetry pattern partially applies (no `Administrator` Owner seeded but the asymmetry pattern on related services is documented).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Extend `configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/owners.md` with an "API and operational details" H2 covering: (1) `POST /api/owners` creation surface with `OwnerFormData` body shape (name + optional roles[]); (2) `OWNER_CREATE` MANAGEMENT-tier permission gate (cross-link `/authorization/permissions`); (3) Name validation gaps — `@NotNull` only, no `@NotBlank` / `@Size` / `@Pattern`; consequence for operators (homoglyph / whitespace / control-character names accepted); (4) Audit-trail absence — cross-link DOC-GAP-083 META; (5) Soft-delete recovery — `owner.name UNIQUE` is non-partial; a soft-deleted Owner permanently holds the name; operators recovering from accidental deletion need direct DB intervention or a migration to apply a partial-unique index; (6) First-login association — no auto-create on first login; operators must either pre-create per-user Owners OR rely on the user-initiated `POST /api/owner_association_request` flow (cross-link the user-owner-association page once one exists). (7) DISABLED bypass — cross-link DOC-GAP-082 META.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-072 (Roles page — same 5-finding shape on sibling surface)
    - DOC-GAP-073 (Policies page — same 5-finding shape on sibling surface)
    - DOC-GAP-074 (201-vs-200 drift on createOwner specifically)
    - DOC-GAP-082 (META — DISABLED bypasses RBAC primary surface)
    - DOC-GAP-083 (META — No-audit-log on RBAC mutations)
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — owners page is partially documented (Owner concept + management UI location), but the API / permission / validation / audit / recovery surface is entirely absent. Operators discovering Owner-management via the UI find the page useful for orientation; operators integrating via API or running incident-response find it incomplete. Severity below DOC-GAP-072 / DOC-GAP-073 because (a) the doc has positive partial coverage of the Owner concept, (b) the Administrator-name reservation asymmetry does NOT directly apply (no seeded Owner Administrator).

  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim re-verified: page documents "permissions into five categories (Data entity, Term, Query Example, Lookup table, and Management)".
    - getResourcePermissions.md verifies: (a) `PolicyTypeDto.java:8-12` enumerates four values: DATA_ENTITY / TERM / QUERY_EXAMPLE / MANAGEMENT (no LOOKUP_TABLE); (b) `SecurityConstants.java:325-354` shows `LOOKUP_TABLE_*` permissions are NO_CONTEXT MANAGEMENT-bucket entries — they cannot be passed as `permission_resource_type` to `GET /api/resource/{type}/{id}/permissions`; (c) A UI page rendering buttons for a single lookup table cannot ask 'what can I do on this lookup table?' through the contextual endpoint — it can only ask 'what management permissions do I have globally?' via the sibling `getNonContextualPermissionsForCurrentUser` path.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Update `configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions.md` to either (a) reframe the category list as "five permission groups, four of which are contextual (per-resource) and one of which (Lookup table) is non-contextual" — or (b) split the category enumeration into "Contextual resource types (DATA_ENTITY / TERM / QUERY_EXAMPLE / MANAGEMENT)" and "Non-contextual permission groups (Lookup table permissions live inside MANAGEMENT)". Preferred: option (b) — explicitly aligns the doc structure with the code's `PolicyTypeDto.hasContext` invariant. Pair with DOC-GAP-076's read-side-discovery H2 on the parent `/authorization.md` page: the `GET /api/resource/{type}/{id}/permissions` endpoint accepts ONLY the four contextual types; lookup-table permission discovery is via the sibling non-contextual endpoint.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-076 (read-side discovery endpoint undocumented — sub-finding)
    - DOC-GAP-078 (LOOKUP_TABLE admin-policy coverage unverified — adjacent gap)
    - Concept "Permission (Authorization)" in concepts.yaml (new batch E)
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — doc-code shape mismatch on a security-relevant enum. Operators reading the categorical structure as 5-categories may attempt `GET /api/resource/LOOKUP_TABLE/{id}/permissions` and receive a `BadUserRequestException` ('Resource type LOOKUP_TABLE does not have context') with no in-page guidance. Severity bounded by the fact that the docs' framing is informationally accurate (lookup-table permissions DO exist as a category), only the structural shape is wrong.

- **DOC-GAP-080**: Search live doc page silent on query syntax — `JooqFTSHelper.tsQuery` splits user input on a single space, appends `:*` to each token, joins with `&`, and passes verbatim to Postgres `to_tsquery(?)`; user queries with tsquery-meaningful metacharacters (`!`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `<->`, `:`) silently re-interpret or yield syntax-error 500s
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__SearchController__controller-method__search.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[1]` + `:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[1]` (MEDIUM) **(NEW batch E)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Search Session].security_aggregate.weaknesses.[1]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/search` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim verdict (re-verified): "Query syntax — No documentation of wildcards, special characters, or Postgres tsquery semantics" + "Maximum query length or pagination limits — Not specified".
    - search.md verifies: `JooqFTSHelper.java:164-168` `tsQuery` splits on `" "`, appends `:*` to each token, joins with `&`. Test cases: (a) `foo bar` → `foo:* & bar:*` — valid; (b) `foo!bar` → `foo!bar:*` — Postgres raises `syntax error in tsquery`; (c) `foo|bar` → `foo|bar:*` — same; (d) `(foo)` → `(foo):*` — same; (e) `foo<->bar` → `foo<->bar:*` — Postgres re-interprets `<->` as the phrase-distance operator silently. `SearchFormData.query` (components.yaml SearchFormData block) carries no `@Size` / `@Pattern` / maxLength. Combined with the absence of rate-limiting (search.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases.[6]), syntax-error queries are a DB-CPU-amplification DoS vector under DISABLED (anonymous) or any authenticated mode.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add a "Query syntax" sub-section to `features/data-discovery/search.md`: "The search query is space-tokenised; each token becomes a Postgres tsquery prefix-match (`token:*`), and tokens are AND-joined. Single-word queries match prefixes (`fo` matches `foo`, `foobar`, etc.). The query is interpreted as a `to_tsquery` argument verbatim — meaning: (a) special characters with tsquery meaning (`!`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `<->`, `:`) NOT preceded by a space will produce a syntax error response; (b) queries containing balanced tsquery expressions will be interpreted by Postgres as those expressions; (c) no escape mechanism is currently exposed — operators needing to search for literal special characters should rely on the facet filters (Tag, Owner, Namespace, etc.) instead." Pair with a maxLength caveat (no limit currently enforced) cross-referencing DOC-GAP-022 (pagination unbounded class).
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-022 (pagination unbounded — same class of input-validation gap)
    - DOC-GAP-079 (search WHO + visibility — adjacent gap on the same page)
    - Concept "Search Session" in concepts.yaml; surfaces a `to_tsquery` syntax-error vector worth a `/log-issue odd-platform` upstream
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — operationally significant for the catalog's primary discovery vector. Users searching for special-character-bearing entity names (database table names with parentheses, hyphenated terms, etc.) get inconsistent results (sometimes 500, sometimes silent re-interpretation, sometimes prefix-glob match). The DoS amplification surface is bounded by deployment-network reach but real under DISABLED.

- **DOC-GAP-081**: Legacy URL `/features/active-platform-features/search` returns 404 — canonical at `/features/data-discovery/search`; 3rd corroborating instance of the legacy-vs-canonical routing-drift cross-cutting pattern (strengthens DOC-GAP-058 META from 2-sidecar to 3-sidecar)
  - **Category**: broken-url
  - **Surfaced by**:
    - `odd-platform__java__SearchController__controller-method__search.md:docs_link_semantic.inferred_docs.[1]` (status 404 verified this session) **(NEW batch E)**
    - `concepts.yaml:entities[Search Session]`
  - **Evidence**:

    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/features/data-discovery/search` 2026-05-12 status 200 — canonical search page renders normally.
    - This is the third instance of the same shape — `/features/active-platform-features/{slug}` 404 with `/features/data-discovery/{slug}` 200 — joining `/active-platform-features/data-collaboration` (batch A; DOC-GAP-035) and `/active-platform-features/notifications` (batch C; DOC-GAP-056). The 2-sidecar pattern in DOC-GAP-058 META is now 3-sidecar; the recommendation for a doc-side audit of ALL legacy paths is strengthened.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Same as DOC-GAP-011 / DOC-GAP-035 / DOC-GAP-056 — included in the doc-side sweep recommended by DOC-GAP-058 META. Add a GitBook redirect rule (`.gitbook.yaml`) for `/features/active-platform-features/search` → `/features/data-discovery/search` since the slug "search" is high-traffic and likely linked from external blog posts / Slack discussions.
  - **Cross-references**:
    - DOC-GAP-035 (data-collaboration legacy 404) + DOC-GAP-056 (notifications legacy 404) — sibling instances
    - DOC-GAP-058 (META — GitBook legacy-vs-canonical routing drift class; THIS finding strengthens to 3-sidecar)
    - DOC-GAP-079 (search WHO/visibility — adjacent gap on the canonical page)
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — broken-URL rubric. The third 3rd-corroborating instance materially strengthens the meta-finding case for a single doc-side audit of all legacy paths.

OR (b) audit the api-reference glossary page for dangling cross-references.

- **DOC-GAP-101**: Popular ranking signal is undocumented externally — `catalog-overview` describes the surface, no page describes the `view_count DESC`-only mechanism, the inflation surface, or the `EXCLUDE_FROM_SEARCH` bypass
  - **Category**: drift (live `catalog-overview` describes the surface but omits the mechanism + the abuse-resistance gap)
  - **Surfaced by**: `getPopular.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[0,1]` + `getPopular.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases[0,1]` + `getPopular.md:security.known_security_gaps[0,1]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `openapi.yaml:877-893` — operation summary "Returns list of the most popular data entities throughout the catalog" — "popular" is undefined.
    - `ReactiveDataEntityRepositoryImpl.java:633` — actual ranking: `.orderBy(DATA_ENTITY.VIEW_COUNT.sort(SortOrder.DESC))` SOLE ordering signal + `id DESC` tiebreaker.
    - `ReactiveDataEntityRepositoryImpl.java:173-180` — `incrementViewCount` is the producer; called from `getDataEntityDetails` on every successful read; no rate-limit, no idempotency.
    - `ReactiveDataEntityRepositoryImpl.java:909-939` — `cteDataEntitySelect` for popular applies `HOLLOW.isFalse()` + soft-delete but NOT `EXCLUDE_FROM_SEARCH` (9 other locations DO apply it).
    - WebFetch `/features/data-discovery/catalog-overview` (2026-05-18, status 200): describes Popular as "the most-viewed or most-used data entities" — no description of how popularity is measured, no warning about manipulability.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Update `data-discovery/catalog-overview` (or add `data-discovery/popular.md`) covering: ranking signal is cumulative-view-count; view counts incremented on every authenticated detail-page read; Popular NOT filtered by `EXCLUDE_FROM_SEARCH`; soft-delete and hollow filters respected; DISABLED-mode anonymous readability.
  - **Severity rationale**: MEDIUM — operators deploying ODD as a multi-team or public-facing catalog need to know the recommendation surface is `view_count`-only and trivially manipulable; today this is invisible. Code-side fix is REFACTOR-201.

- **DOC-GAP-065**: DataCollaboration `sending-messages-retry-count: 0` is accepted by `@PostConstruct` validator (`< 0` check is strict) but docs imply minimum is 1 — semantic edge case undocumented
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**: `odd-platform__java__org_opendatadiscovery_oddplatform_datacollaboration_config__config-properties-class__DataCollaborationProperties.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[2]` (severity LOW per sidecar) **(NEW batch D)**
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/odd-platform` 2026-05-12 status 200 — verbatim: "`datacollaboration.sending-messages-retry-count`: ... Cannot be less than zero. Defaults to `3`." The "cannot be less than zero" phrasing implies values >= 0 are accepted; "Defaults to 3" implies typical use is >= 1.
    - DataCollaborationProperties.md verifies code: `DataCollaborationProperties.java:14-20` — validator is `if (sendingMessagesRetryCount < 0)` (strict `<`, not `<=`). Value `0` is accepted; `shouldRetry()` check uses strict `<` again, meaning `retry-count: 0` produces "try once, no retry" behaviour (the message is attempted once and either succeeds or is marked failed, with NO retry).
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add a single-sentence clarification to the Enable Data Collaboration section: "Setting `datacollaboration.sending-messages-retry-count: 0` is accepted and means 'attempt once, no retry on failure' (the message is sent once; if delivery fails, it is marked failed immediately with no retry attempts). Setting `1` means 'attempt once, retry once on failure'."
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-064 (lock-id collision); DOC-GAP-071 (partial-home).
  - **Severity rationale**: LOW — semantic edge case; the operational impact is minor (operators setting `0` likely intend "no retry" anyway). Docs accuracy improvement.

- **DOC-GAP-078**: Administrator policy's effective scope on `LOOKUP_TABLE_*` permissions depends on `PolicyPermissionExtractor`'s handling of `'ALL'` on the MANAGEMENT type — unverified whether `'ALL'` expands to every LOOKUP_TABLE_* constant; if not, the seeded Administrator effectively cannot manage lookup tables despite being the platform's full-permissions role
  - **Category**: drift
  - **Surfaced by**: `odd-platform__java__PermissionController__controller-method__getResourcePermissions.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings.[2]` (severity MEDIUM per sidecar; tagged LOW here pending verification at a sibling sidecar) **(NEW batch E)**
  - **Evidence**:
    - WebFetch `https://docs.opendatadiscovery.org/configuration-and-deployment/enable-security/authorization/permissions` 2026-05-12 status 200 — page enumerates 5 categories including Lookup table; the seeded Administrator role is described as "administrator, who has all permissions" on `/authorization/roles` (200).
    - getResourcePermissions.md notes: "Live `/authorization/permissions` page lists Lookup table as a permission category, but the seeded Administrator policy (`V0_0_56__add_predefined_roles_and_policies.sql:1-31` + `V0_0_88__add_query_example_policy.sql:1-11`) covers DATA_ENTITY / TERM / QUERY_EXAMPLE / MANAGEMENT only — no LOOKUP_TABLE statement was added in a corresponding migration. The Administrator built-in role's effective scope on lookup tables is whatever the LOOKUP_TABLE_* permissions resolve to under the MANAGEMENT statement's `'permissions': ['ALL']` — which depends on whether `policyPermissionExtractor` treats `'ALL'` on the MANAGEMENT type as expanding to every LOOKUP_TABLE_* constant. This is unverified at this node (it lives in `PolicyPermissionExtractor`, outside this method's scope)."
  - **Proposed doc action**: Two-step. (1) Verify behaviour at the next file-analyser pass on `PolicyPermissionExtractor` — does `'ALL'` on MANAGEMENT expand to LOOKUP_TABLE_* constants? (2) Based on the answer, update `/authorization/roles.md` Administrator description to explicitly state "the seeded Administrator role has all permissions including lookup-table management" (if confirmed) OR add a Known-limitations admonition (if not — operators would need a custom policy adding LOOKUP_TABLE_*  permissions). The doc gap is BOTH: the docs do not clarify what "all permissions" means for the seeded Administrator, AND the underlying code behaviour requires verification.
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-076 (read-side discovery undocumented); DOC-GAP-077 (4-vs-5 enum/category drift); concepts.yaml entity "Permission (Authorization)".
  - **Severity rationale**: LOW — semantic-completeness question; out-of-scope for current sidecars to fully verify. Pending a `PolicyPermissionExtractor` enrichment, the gap remains classified LOW. Could be promoted to MEDIUM if the answer is "no — Administrator does NOT effectively cover LOOKUP_TABLE_*" (operators would be surprised).

#### Batch 2026-05-13-G new LOW finding (DOC-GAP-103)

- **DOC-GAP-103**: LOGIN_FORM and LDAP both produce `provider=null` in `USER_OWNER_MAPPING` — undocumented cross-mode user-identity bleed during auth-mode migrations
  - **Category**: drift (operational migration caveat absent on the Authorization / User-owner-association doc page)
  - **Surfaced by**: `getMyObjects.md:bugs_limitations_corner_cases[6]` + `getMyObjects.md:concepts.invariants[2]` + `getMyObjects.md:docs_link_semantic.doc_drift_findings[1]`
  - **Evidence**:
    - `AuthIdentityProviderImpl.java:24-35` — only `OAuth2AuthenticationToken` produces a non-null `provider`; LOGIN_FORM and LDAP both emit `null`.
    - `ReactiveUserOwnerMappingRepositoryImpl.java:116-127` — `getConditions` clause matches `PROVIDER IS NULL` AND null-provider path — LOGIN_FORM `alice` and LDAP `alice` both resolve against the same `USER_OWNER_MAPPING` row.
    - Operator switching `auth.type` from LOGIN_FORM to LDAP with overlapping usernames inherits prior LOGIN_FORM users' owner-mappings.
  - **Proposed doc action**: Add migration-caveat admonition on `enable-security/authorization` (or user-owner-association deep-dive) explaining: (a) `OWNER_MAPPING.provider` null under LOGIN_FORM and LDAP; (b) switching auth.type mid-deployment with overlapping usernames may inadvertently re-use mappings; (c) recommended migration procedure includes auditing `USER_OWNER_MAPPING` for cross-mode collisions.
  - **Cross-references**: DOC-GAP-046 / DOC-GAP-049 (OAuth2-doc gaps) — same family.
  - **Severity rationale**: LOW — narrow operational case (only matters during auth-mode migration); behaviour not unsafe by default but surprising.

### Batch 2026-05-13-G summary

Batch G is the **dataEntity-controller-method confirmation pass**. Three of the five sidecars (addDataEntityTerm, upsertDataEntityInternalDescription, createDataEntityTagsRelations) are write-surfaces whose live permissions-page wording is correct but whose blast-radius / scope-asymmetry / drift consequences are entirely absent — same META-pattern as DOC-GAP-077 (re-verified live this session: status 200, no path / asymmetry warnings). Two of the five (getMyObjects, getPopular) are read-surfaces where the live `catalog-overview` page does describe the user-visible feature (re-verified status 200) but stops at the surface — the inflation surface, the EXCLUDE_FROM_SEARCH bypass, the empty-Flux failure mode, and the LOGIN_FORM/LDAP provider-null collision are silent. The single highest-priority new HIGH finding is **DOC-GAP-099** (`getMyObjectsWithUpstream`/`Downstream` summary literally describes the inverse semantic) — a third-party consumer reading the OpenAPI spec is misled about whose data they're seeing in a multi-tenant deployment.

**Recommended triage order**: DOC-GAP-099 (spec drift, security-impact) → DOC-GAP-096 (description-XSS surface absent on every doc page) → DOC-GAP-098 (replace-all-as-create) → DOC-GAP-097 (silent-200-on-missing-id) → DOC-GAP-100/101/102/103 (operational caveats).

## RETRACTION — DOC-GAP-088 (hand-annotation, 2026-06-08, per DOC-338)

DOC-GAP-088's conclusion ("`DataEntityMapperImpl.applyStatus` resets `statusUpdatedAt` on every transition, so the 30-day soft-delete TTL never fires / soft-deleted entities never hard-delete / the platform has no functioning row-by-age retention path") is **INVERTED** and is retracted. The `applyStatus` always-false guard is real but affects only **non-`DELETED`** transitions. The transition **to** `DELETED` runs through a separate soft-delete path (`ReactiveDataEntityRepositoryImpl.getDeleteChangedFields`, `:112-114`) that stamps `STATUS_UPDATED_AT = now()` correctly, and `DataEntityHousekeepingJob` (`:73-77`) purges on that column — so the 30-day hard-delete purge **IS active and irreversible** (incl. S3/MinIO objects) on a default install. See DOC-338 for the full code-verified proof; DOC-191 / DOC-293 are superseded.
